## [2026-06-28] ingest | Pseudo-Dionysius, *On the Divine Names* and *Mystical Theology* (Parker 1897)

Source: `raw/Dionysius the Areopagite, Works (1897) [ThML].txt` — Parker translation, Vol. 1 (1897).
Lines 166–512 (Parker's preface to *Divine Names* + Caput I–XIII of *Divine Names* + Parker's
preface to *Mystical Theology* + Caput I–V of *Mystical Theology*). **Primary patristic text
(theology/mysticism).** Scope: *On the Divine Names* complete (Caput I–XIII) and *Mystical
Theology* complete (Caput I–V). Read in full for this scope (agent range 166–512).

Pages filled: `wiki/texts/on-divine-names.md` (all Caput summaries, Key Theological Positions,
Relation to Neoplatonism, Reception and Influence); `wiki/texts/mystical-theology.md` (all Caput
summaries, Key Theological Positions). Pages extended: `wiki/concepts/apophatic-theology.md`
(Pseudo-Dionysius section added); `wiki/concepts/theosis-divine-nature.md` (Divine Names material
added to existing Pseudo-Dionysius section); `wiki/concepts/evil-as-privation.md` (Pseudo-Dionysius
section added with verbatim extracts from DN IV.XIX–XXXV).

Key verbatim anchors: "beneficent progressions" (Proodoi) = DN I; evil "parasitical subsistence"
(DN IV.XXXI); "knowing nothing, knowing above mind" (MT II); "above every abstraction" (MT V); the
three movements of souls — circular, spiral, straight (DN IV.VIII–IX); divine darkness as
"super-luminous gloom" (MT I.I). Parker's translator framing: authentic 1st-c. apostolic text;
Dionysius as "Master not Pupil" to Proclus/Plotinus.

Scope declared: lines 166–512 — read in full.

Source type: scholarship/primary text (complete first Western translation of Zhu Xi's Yijing commentary). Scope: Introduction (lines 1–658, read in full) + representative hexagram sample: Hexagrams 1–3 (lines 659–~1100, read in full) + Xici zhuan / Treatise on the Appended Remarks Parts A–B (lines 6830–~7400, read in full) + Shuogua first three sections (lines 7640–7739, read in full). Notes, Bibliography, Index, and Hexagrams 4–64 declared out of scope (repetitive commentary format fully documented by sample).

**Key contributions**: (1) Zhu Xi's hermeneutical key: "the Yi was originally created for divination" — against Wang Bi (yili only) and Cheng Yi (moralized text); synthesis of xiangshu (Shao Yong) and yili. (2) Bronze Age Zhouyi vs. Ten Wings: the original text was statecraft divination; the Ten Wings moralized it (junzi: 15 times in Zhouyi, 87 in Ten Wings). (3) Xici cosmology: taiji → Two Modes → Four Images → Eight Trigrams; "The alternation of yin and yang is called the Way"; *sheng sheng* (generation/regeneration = meaning of change). (4) Fu hexagram (24) as theological heart: "In Fu is seen the mind of Heaven and Earth" — cosmic incipience linking the natural and moral order. (5) Zhu Xi's *Zhouyi benyi* structure: Nine Diagrams + Hexagram commentary + Xici + Shuogua + Zagua + Shiyi; original arrangement (appendixes separate) vs. collated (Adler's choice).

**Pages created (2)**: `texts/yijing.md`, `scholarship/adler-original-meaning-yijing.md`. **Pages updated (3)**: `commentators/zhu-xi.md` (Yi commentary hermeneutic + major works update), `traditions/confucianism/confucianism.md` (yijing added to canon_core), `index.md`. `outstanding sources.md` updated.

## 2026-06-10 ingest | de Lubac, *Medieval Exegesis: The Four Senses of Scripture*, Vols. 2 and 3

Source type: scholarship (monograph). **Vol. 2**: Chs. 6–10, lines 140–1394 — read in full. **Vol. 3**: Chs. 1–6, lines 1–2191 (Notes and Index not within scope) — read in full.

**Vol. 2 key contributions**: (1) Christian allegory not derived from pagan allegorism — Pauline warrant (Gal 4:24); allegoria *facti et dicti*, the historical events themselves are figures. (2) *Historia* as permanent foundation, not scaffolding; "first lay the foundation of history." (3) Content of allegory = always Christ and the Church, three dimensions: Future / Interior / Celestial. (4) Twofold tropology: simple moral + mystical (interiorization of the Mystery of Christ); *quotidie*. (5) Bernard of Clairvaux as "last of the Fathers." (6) Twofold anagogy: eschatological + contemplative; the three-advent structural key; Jerusalem as the paradigm symbol.

**Vol. 3 key contributions**: (1) Refutation of the Spicq/Mandonnet/Smalley "precursors of literalism" thesis — Berno, Bruno of Segni, Rupert, Alcuin, Stephen Harding all shown to be thoroughgoing allegorists. (2) The "Non tuo sensui" principle was anti-heretical, not anti-allegorical; Christian allegory is the most objective reading. (3) Jerome was never invoked against Gregory — always paired. (4) Hugh of Saint Victor: conservative defender of the synthesis, not proto-literalist; his Ark descriptions mystical, not historical-critical; his emphasis on history defensive against Abelardian rationalism. (5) Victorine school: Andrew genuine exception; Godfrey, Absalon, Adam — traditional; Gautier anomalously narrow. (6) Joachim of Flora: the *concordia* = typic understanding (literal parallelism between OT history and Church history), not spiritual understanding; Three Ages (Father/Son/Spirit); the one genuine structural aberration; Thomas and Bonaventure directly opposed him; personally orthodox; de Lubac's closing: two equally deadly errors (Spirit outpacing Christ / Christ restraining Spirit).

**Pages updated (2)**: `scholarship/de-lubac-medieval-exegesis.md` (Vols. 2–3 content + coverage ledger), `concepts/fourfold-sense.md` (substance of each sense; mystical tropology; anagogy; three-advent key). **Pages created (3)**: `commentators/hugh-of-saint-victor.md`, `commentators/richard-of-saint-victor.md`, `commentators/joachim-of-flora.md`. **Index updated**: de Lubac scholarship entry expanded; new Medieval Christian Tradition commentators section added.

(8) **Abhidhamma cognitive series** (*vīthi*): life-continuum → adverting → five-door → receiving → investigating → determining → seven impulsions → registration → life-continuum. (9) **Suttanta vs. Abhidhamma** distinction acknowledged but not hierarchically ranked (e.g. on the number of liberations). (10) **Hermeneutical method**: systematic-harmonizing; resolves canonical tensions by definition; uses the Paṭisambhidāmagga as his primary analytical source; records divergent elder opinions without adjudication.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Matt, Daniel C. (trans.). *The Zohar: Pritzker Edition, Volume I*. Stanford University Press, 2004.

Source type: primary text of [[kabbalah|Kabbalah]] / Jewish mysticism (presented in the wiki as a primary text of the Kabbalistic tradition, though formally a mystical Torah commentary), in Daniel C. Matt's annotated English translation from a reconstructed critical Aramaic text (~20 manuscripts collated against the Mantua/Cremona printed editions). Volume I covers the Haqdamah (Proem) and Parashat Be-Reshit (Gen 1:1–6:8) and Parashat Noaḥ (Gen 6:9–11:32), with a scholarly introduction by Arthur Green. **This is the wiki's first Jewish-mysticism source — a first-order scope expansion** beyond the previously-dominant rabbinic-halakhic Judaism, evangelical-Protestant Christianity, and patristic/early-Christianity coverage.

Key contributions and takeaways: (1) **Two-tier Godhead** — the utterly unknowable [[ein-sof|Ein Sof]] (Infinite) vs. the ten [[sefirot|sefirot]] (dynamic, knowable, gendered emanations) that constitute the inner life of God; the biblical/liturgical "blessed Holy One" is Tif'eret (6th sefirah), not Ein Sof. (2) **Theogonic re-reading of Genesis 1:1** — *bereshit bara Elohim* re-parsed so that *Elohim* (= Binah) is the object created "with Beginning" (= Ḥokhmah) by the unnamed Ein Sof; creation as **emanation**, not *creatio ex nihilo*. (3) **The divine feminine** — [[shekhinah|Shekhinah]]/Malkhut as full divine persona, Bride, Moon, Assembly of Israel; her union (*zivvuga qaddisha*) with Tif'eret as the central theurgical event; her exile = Israel's exile. (4) **Theodicy of the [[sitra-ahra|Sitra Aḥra]]** — evil as the parasitic shadow of Gevurah's untempered excess, originating *within* the divine, not a second principle; Samael and Lilith. (5) **Theurgy** — *mitsvot* performed with *kavvanah* maintain the sefirotic union; human action completes something incomplete in God. (6) **Sod-level hermeneutics** ([[pardes|PaRDeS]]) — sefirotic correspondence, letter mysticism, theogonic reading; "Woe to the one who says Torah comes to tell mere tales." (7) **Patriarchs as sefirot** — [[abraham|Abraham]]=Ḥesed, [[isaac|Isaac]]=Gevurah ("Fear of Isaac"), Jacob=Tif'eret, Joseph=Yesod, David=Malkhut; [[moses|Moses]] uniquely prophesies from Tif'eret ("bridegroom of the Shekhinah," the "clear mirror"). (8) **Authorship** — attributed to the 2nd-c. Tanna [[shimon-bar-yochai|Shim'on bar Yoḥai]] but, on the critical consensus established by Scholem, composed by [[moses-de-leon|Moses de León]] and his Castilian circle c. 1280–1300 (see [[zohar-authorship|controversy]]).

Contradictions flagged: the Zoharic/rabbinic ambivalence toward [[noah|Noah]] (righteous only "in his generation"; failed to intercede) vs. the NT exaltation of Noah as unqualified "herald of righteousness" (recorded side by side on the Noah page); the Zoharic emanation cosmology vs. the *creatio ex nihilo* reading defended on the [[genesis|Genesis]] page; the Kabbalistic theodicy (evil from within the divine) vs. the [[theodicy|Job/theodicy]] and Revelation frameworks (evil as external adversary or moral trial).

Source type: NICNT (New International Commentary on the New Testament; final editorial task for Gordon D. Fee as general editor; replacing F. F. Bruce's 1964 NICNT volume). (2) **Genre**: Christian synagogue homily (*logos tēs paraklēseōs*, 13:22); adapted with Hellenistic rhetorical resources; first cited by 1 Clement in Rome (~AD 96); "those from Italy" (13:24) supports Roman destination; pre-AD 70 date most probable; (3) **Chiastic structure**: I (1:1–4:13, "Very Short History of the Disobedient") || II (4:14–10:18, Christ's High Priesthood, center) || III (10:19–12:29, "History of the Faithful People of God"); within I and III further chiasm based on Sinai and Promised Land imagery; Ps 110:1 as the structural spine unifying all three sections; (4) **OT use**: 28 passages quoted (32 occurrences); key pillars are Ps 110:1, Ps 110:4, Ps 40:6–8, Jer 31:31–34; the OT is structured by two coordinated uses — divine proclamations from Psalms/Prophets announce fulfillment; Pentateuchal descriptions establish typological pattern (Aaron as type, not Melchizedek); (5) **Anti-neo-Platonic worldview**: rejects Spicq/Thompson reading that Hebrews shares Philo's spatial dualism; follows Williamson (1970), Hurst (1990), Laansma (1997), Adams — Jewish apocalyptic (concrete heavenly world + futuristic two-age eschatology) is the proper framework; something is "better" in Hebrews because of what Christ accomplished, not because it is metaphysically superior; Eisele's extreme (removing Christ's return from Hebrews) rejected; (6) **Warning passages (6:4–8; 10:26–31)**: Wesleyan-Arminian reading — five participles describe genuine Christian experience; apostasy described is real and completed; "impossible" is grounded in God's character (no alternate provision after definitively rejecting the once-for-all atonement), parallel to "impossible for God to lie" (6:18); those anxious for their condition are not apostate; (7) **Melchizedek**: Aaron (not Melchizedek) is the type of Christ's priesthood; Melchizedek provides Scriptural evidence (Gen 14 + Ps 110:4) for Christ's eternal, non-Aaronic character; Melchizedek is a human figure whose unique role in Gen 14 (without genealogy, without recorded beginning or end) foreshadows the Son's eternity.

Source type: PNTC (Pillar New Testament Commentary; D. A. Carson general editor). Covenant Theological Seminary (PCA); formerly Trinity Evangelical Divinity School. Method: grammatico-historical with intensive synchronic lexicography (Josephus, Philo, Epictetus, Apostolic Fathers); theocentric frame (God/Christ language = ~4% of total words in each PE, the dominant topical cluster); pastoral-theological orientation via Oden's eight rubrics; global Christianity hermeneutic as corrective to Western-dominated pseudonymity consensus.

Key contributions: (1) Pauline authorship defended against the dominant Western scholarly consensus; Yarbrough's most distinctive moves are recruiting Adolf Schlatter (70+ Pauline parallels, largely untranslated) and the "global Christianity" argument (53% of world's Protestants will be African by 2050; the global church reads the PE as Pauline); (2) Ethics argument against pseudonymity: a pseudonymous author swearing "I am telling the truth, I am not lying" (1 Tim 2:7) and invoking God in doxology while depicting imminent martyrdom (2 Tim 4:17–18) is engaged in deception bordering on blasphemy, not exercising a neutral literary convention; (3) 1 Tim 2:12 (*authenteō*): Wolters and Burk confirm the verb is neutral/positive — "the positive exercise of authority over men, not its abuse or wrongful assumption"; Köstenberger's grammatical construction shows both "teach" and "exercise authority" are positive activities; the prohibition targets the congregational pastoral office, not women's teaching in all settings; grounded in Gen 2 (creation order) + Gen 3 (Eve first deceived); (4) Titus 2:13 ("our great God and Savior, Jesus Christ"): Granville Sharp's canon + Wallace confirms this as an explicit Christ-as-God text; (5) 2 Tim 3:16 (*theopneustos*): "God-breathed" stresses *origin*, not manner; Wallace's grammar forecloses the NEB's "every inspired Scripture has its use" — all Scripture is equally God-breathed; (6) distinctive PE vocabulary (*hygiainōn didaskalia*, *eusebeia*, *pistos ho logos*, *epiphaneia*) all attested in contemporaneous sources — not evidence of non-Pauline authorship; (7) Titus 2:11–14 two-epiphanies framework: past epiphany (incarnation, 2:11) + future epiphany (parousia, 2:13) as brackets of Christian life.

Pages created/updated: texts/1-timothy (new), texts/2-timothy (new), texts/titus (new), commentators/robert-yarbrough (new), controversies/pastoral-epistles-authorship (new), controversies/1-tim-2-12-women-teaching (new). Wiki: 6 new pages.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Weima, Jeffrey A. D. *1–2 Thessalonians*. BECNT. Baker Academic, 2014.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Calvin Theological Seminary; PhD Wycliffe College Toronto; 20+ years preparing the commentary; cochaired the SNTS Thessalonian Correspondence seminar. Method: epistolary-literary approach (each passage analyzed under form, boundaries, and structure); grammatico-historical exegesis; strong emphasis on anti-imperial context; pastoral-consolatory reading.

Key contributions: (1) city of Thessalonica — Via Egnatia, free city, politarch office (~70 inscriptions, 28 from Thessalonica, vindicating Acts 17:6), Cabirus cult, heavy imperial ideology, religious pluralism; (2) 1 Thess: unanimous Pauline authorship; church founded in Jason's house via workshop evangelism; 2:13–16 interpolation debate — Weima defends authenticity (four arguments against interpolation); (3) 2 Thess: sustained defense of Pauline authorship against Trilling's four arguments (tone, 3:17, eschatological contradiction, literary dependence); temple reference (2:4) as fifth argument — a post-70 forger would not presuppose a standing temple; (4) 1 Thess 4:13–18 — pastoral problem: deceased believers feared at disadvantage at the parousia; *apantēsis* is a technical term for Hellenistic formal reception (Peterson 1930); believers escort descending Christ to earth, not fly to heaven; *harpazō* inverts funerary usage where death "snatches" the living; dispensationalist "secret rapture" reading rejected; (5) 2 Thess 2: the restrainer (*to katechon* / *ho katechōn*) = archangel Michael (Weima's preferred identification) from Daniel 10–12 allusion — Daniel's neuter→masculine shift; Michael "stands aside" (Dan 12:1a LXX, *parerchomai*) triggering tribulation; detailed chronological parallel table; Weima counsels tentativeness; (6) the man of lawlessness identified from Daniel 10–12 (Dan 11:36 self-exaltation, Dan 11:31 temple desecration, Dan 12:10 lawlessness); destroyed by Christ's breath (Isa 11:4 allusion).

Pages created/updated: texts/1-thessalonians (new), texts/2-thessalonians (new), commentators/jeffrey-weima (new), controversies/2-thessalonians-authorship (new), controversies/restrainer-2-thess-2 (new), controversies/parousia-1-thess-4 (new), concepts/antichrist (updated — sources: 2→3, man of lawlessness section added). ## 2026-06-06 ingest | Moo, Douglas J. *Galatians*. BECNT. Baker Academic, 2013.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Evangelical; Wheaton College/TEDS; PhD St. Andrews; chaired NIV 2011 revision committee. Key contributions: (1) slightly favors South Galatian destination (cities of first missionary journey; possibly earliest Pauline letter, c. AD 48); (2) defends three-level logic of Paul's anti-Torah argument — salvation-historical (Torah era ended with Christ) + ethnic/social (Torah excluded Gentiles) + anthropological (human inability to do Torah; Gal. 3:10 syllogism); Westerholm's summation: "What is wrong with humanity that Judaism cannot remedy?"; (3) justification in Galatians is primarily future-oriented — about "ultimate justification" in the final judgment, not just initial acceptance; (4) defends objective genitive on *pistis Christou* ("faith in Christ," not "faithfulness of Christ") against Hays/Campbell; (5) "the law of Christ" (6:2) is the new covenant's own ethical authority, not the OT law as fulfilled; (6) union with Christ is more fundamental than justification; both justification and transformation are products of "in Christ" (Calvin's double gift). Most sustained evangelical NPP dialogue in the wiki.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Seifrid, Mark A. *The Second Letter to the Corinthians*. PNTC. Eerdmans, 2014.

Source type: PNTC (Pillar New Testament Commentary; D. A. Carson, general editor; Eerdmans). Evangelical; SBTS; Lutheran-evangelical synthesis. Seifrid is a leading evangelical justification scholar. Motto: *Verbum crucis, virtus Dei* ("The word of the cross is the power of God"). Method: theologia crucis as hermeneutical key; defends letter's unity against partition theories; minimalist mirror-reading of opponents. Key contributions: (1) single purpose thesis — the whole letter legitimates Paul's apostolic authority; (2) the apostle is "Christian existence in large-screen display" — the form of apostolic life (weakness → power, dying → rising) is the form of all Christian life; (3) opponents have no unified theological program — they share only a practical theology of visible success (Paul's term: *theologia gloriae*); (4) 2 Cor 3 (Moses/new covenant) is addressed to the Corinthians, not opponents — the veil is a universal human condition removed only in Christ; (5) new creation (5:17) is personal but necessarily implies cosmic renewal; (6) the great exchange (5:21) is the most compressed NT formulation of substitutionary atonement; (7) salvation is never a possession but an abiding communication between God and the human being; sanctification is passive before active — God-driven not purpose-driven.
Pages created/updated: texts/2-corinthians (new), commentators/mark-seifrid (new), concepts/theology-of-the-cross (new), concepts/new-creation (new), controversies/pauls-opponents-2-corinthians (new). Wiki: 177 pages.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Ciampa, Roy E., and Brian S. Rosner. *The First Letter to the Corinthians*. PNTC. Eerdmans, 2010.

Source type: PNTC (Pillar New Testament Commentary; D. A. Carson, general editor; Eerdmans). Co-authored; evangelical. Ciampa (Gordon-Conwell) and Rosner (Moore/Ridley, Australia). Method: grammatico-historical with primary biblical-theological / OT-first orientation; resists classical rhetoric as interpretive framework; reads 1 Corinthians through Deuteronomy and Isaiah. Key contributions: (1) social values thesis — root cause of all Corinthian problems = infiltration of Roman Corinthian status-consciousness, not overrealized eschatology or Gnosticism; (2) letter structured around two Gentile vices: sexual immorality (chs. 5–7) and idolatry (chs. 8–14), each with negative + positive treatment — "Flee X / Glorify God in Y"; (3) Deuteronomy as canonical analogue (Paul = new Moses; new exodus ethics); (4) Isaiah as primary OT source for wisdom/foolishness of the cross (chs. 1–4) and for Paul's eschatological-herald self-understanding; (5) Malachi 1:11 echo in 1:2 ("in every place") as fulfillment of worldwide Gentile worship; (6) eschatological temple as load-bearing ecclesiological concept grounding all ethical instruction; (7) glory of God as ultimate goal — the letter climaxes in 15:28 ("God may be all in all"); (8) bodily resurrection as the consummation and the foundation of ethics (15:58).

Key contributions: (1) preamble = 1:1–5 (not prologue 1:1–18), narrative begins at 1:6; (2) "light" not "Logos/Word" as dominant image; (3) public ministry (chs. 2–12) as extended trial/judgment; (4) Johannine determinism (rebirth precedes faith; Father's initiative is primary); (5) Beloved Disciple probably not John son of Zebedee; possibly a brother of Jesus; (6) Advocate (not Beloved Disciple) is the Gospel's ultimate truth guarantor; (7) John independent of written Synoptics.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Evangelical; Beeson Divinity School (Samford University). Method: grammatico-historical exegesis with extensive patristic engagement, OT background analysis (especially Isaiah and Letter of Aristeas), and imperial-cult awareness. Key contributions: (1) Careful Pauline authorship defense — stylistic concentration explained by Roman imprisonment dictation; Ignatius parallel (identical Tychicus commendation in two simultaneous letters) as stronger evidence for Pauline than pseudonymous authorship; c. AD 62. (2) "In Ephesus" (1:1) textual problem — absent from 𝔓46, א, B; Thielman defends inclusion by arguing that omission produces "almost unintelligible Greek" and parallels scribal universalization of Romans. (3) Middle wall (*mesotoichon tou phragmou*, 2:14) = the Mosaic law as social barrier, supported by Letter of Aristeas 139's use of *phragmos* for the law's national-separating function. (4) Imperial cult counter-narrative: Augustus's calendar redesign in Asia c. 9 BC; Ephesian vocabulary of *kyrios*, *sōtēr*, *euangelia*, "all things under his feet" as direct counter-claims. (5) *Berakah* structure of 1:3–14: the 202-word sentence as Jewish-form benedictory prayer, with four *en Christō/en hō*-divided sections; closest structural parallel at 4Q434. (6) Church as cosmic actor (3:10): the church's Jew-Gentile unity is a proclamation addressed to the powers, demonstrating the doom of their division-sustaining work. (7) *Mystērion* = hidden divine plan (Jewish apocalyptic *rāz* background, not mystery cult); dominant Ephesian content = Gentile inclusion as full co-heirs (3:6's three *syn-* compounds). (8) Principalities and powers cosmology: *kosmokratores* (Eph 6:12; first occurrence in Greek literature); Clinton Arnold's Ephesian magic background; armor of God's six pieces drawn primarily from Isaiah (11:5; 59:17). (9) Household code (5:21–6:9): complementarian reading; 5:21 introduces but does not equalize asymmetry; *kephalē* = authority not source (Grudem vs. Bedale); husband's role defined by Christ's self-sacrificial love. (10) Eph 2:19–22 temple: *oikonomia*-to-*oikos*-to-*naos* progression threading through chs. 1–3.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | McKnight, Scot. *The Letter to the Colossians*. NICNT. Eerdmans, 2018.

Source type: NICNT (New International Commentary on the New Testament, Eerdmans). Post-conservative evangelical; Northern Seminary (Lombard, IL). Method: grammatico-historical with heavy Second Temple Jewish background (1 Enoch, Philo, DSS, 4 Ezra), close engagement with NPP scholarship (Dunn, Wright, Barclay, Campbell, Gorman), and consistent ecclesiological focus. Key contributions: (1) **"Known-to-unknown" methodology critique** — the most original recent contribution to Pauline authorship discussions: since no Pauline letter is "pure Paul" (all co-authored, dictated, situationally varied), there is no baseline against which Colossians can be found deviant; Colossians is "Pauline as much as Galatians." (2) **Halakic mystics thesis** — the Colossian opponents are Jewish Christians combining Torah observance (food laws, Sabbath, calendar) with asceticism aimed at heavenly ascent and angelic-liturgy participation; not pagan syncretism; insiders not outsiders; Colossae connects to Galatia (same stoicheia, same practices). (3) **Christology as port of entry** — Paul's theology enters through the Damascus Christophany, not soteriology; narrative sequence: theocracy → monarchy → christocracy → eschatological handover. (4) **Christoformity** (not cruciformity) — formation into the whole Christ-event (cross + resurrection + ascension), not just the cross; corrects Gorman. (5) **Christological anthropology** (1:15) — Adam was modeled on Christ the prior Eikōn-template, not Christ on Adam; christological anthropology, not Adamic Christology. (6) **Powers as polluted structures** — powers in Col 1:16 and 2:15 are earthly, systemic manifestations of cosmic/angelic rebellion (not a celestial hierarchy of beings); thrones/dominions are heavenly; rulers/authorities are their earthly servants; the cross defeats the *system*. (7) **Household code as anti-Aristotle** — the husband is never told to lead; submission is grounded in Christoformity ("fitting in the Lord"), not hierarchy; complementarian readings are "more Aristotelian than Christian." Direct evangelical-vs-evangelical contrast with Thielman. (8) **Ephesian imprisonment** — favors Ephesus (c. AD 52–57) over Rome based on distance (Onesimus), Aristarchus (Acts 19:29), Philemon 22. (9) **Realized eschatology as cosmic vision** — not a shift from Paul but the future "swallowed into a cosmic vision"; the immanent making immediate the future. (10) **Supra-imperial not anti-imperial** — endorses Galinsky over Walsh/Maier; warns against circular anti-empire hermeneutics.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Moo, Douglas J. *The Letter of James*. PNTC. Eerdmans, 2000.

Source type: PNTC (Pillar New Testament Commentary, Eerdmans; ed. D. A. Carson). Evangelical, grammatical-historical with theological integration; a substantial revision/near-doubling of Moo's 1985 TNTC volume. Moo's second source in the wiki (after BECNT Galatians), extending his coverage from the Pauline corpus into the General Epistles. Key contributions: (1) **Spiritual wholeness as the letter's central concern** — *teleios* (wholeness/maturity) vs. *dipsychos* (double-mindedness, a word James apparently coined); the emotional climax (4:4–10, "you adulteresses") as the heart of the letter; resists both Dibelius's "chaotic paraenesis" and over-structured rhetorical schemes. (2) **Traditional authorship + early date** — James the Lord's brother, middle 40s, possibly the earliest NT document; answers the four critical objections (kinship-silence, good Greek, "liberal" Torah, the Paul relationship); presses a counter-objection that ancient pseudepigrapha were not accepted as authoritative when known to be forged. (3) **The James/Paul justification resolution** — *dikaioō* differs: Paul = initial forensic declaration at conversion; James = ultimate vindication at the final judgment, taking works into account; James's "faith alone" = bogus word-only/demonic faith (2:19), not Pauline faith; rejects both "demonstrate-to-be-righteous" and "two justifications"; integrates James's apparent synergism into Pauline monergism (works = Spirit-produced fruit). (4) **Covenantal nomism** discussed and qualified (diversity; covenant ≠ guaranteed salvation; final-judgment synergism). (5) Exegetical decisions: divine jealousy (not human envy) in 4:5; **anointing as a consecration symbol** (not medicinal/sacramental/exorcistic) in 5:14; physical illness + non-mechanical "prayer of faith" in 5:14–16, with a critique of "enough faith" healing theology; voluntary-oath (not legal-oath) reading of 5:12. (6) Genre: rejects over-classifying James as wisdom; prefers "sermon/homily in letter form"; notes the decline of Dibelius's "paraenesis."

Pages created: texts/james (new); figures/james-the-just (new — the wiki's first figure page); controversies/james-paul-justification (new); controversies/james-authorship (new); controversies/anointing-healing-james-5 (new); concepts/covenantal-nomism (new); concepts/double-mindedness (new).

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Jobes, Karen H. *1 Peter*. BECNT. Baker Academic, 2005.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Evangelical; Westmont College (later Wheaton); Ph.D. Westminster (Philadelphia); Septuagint specialist (co-author with Moisés Silva of *Invitation to the Septuagint*). The wiki's first solo-authored woman commentator. Three self-described distinctive contributions: (1) **Roman colonization theory** — the recipients were not indigenous Asians evangelized in situ (no historical evidence) but Christians converted elsewhere (probably Rome) and *displaced to Asia Minor*, plausibly via Claudius's colonization (he founded/upgraded colonies in all five regions of 1:1) and/or the Claudian expulsion of Jews/Christians from Rome (Suetonius *Claudius* 25; Acts 18:2); explains the rapid arrival of Christianity, the absent founding evangelist, the readers' LXX literacy, and the literal force of "foreigners and resident aliens." (2) **LXX-as-matrix** — Peter cites the OT in context (not proof-texting); the Greek OT is the primary literary/theological context. (3) **Syntax criticism / bilingual interference** — the Greek's syntax (not its rhetoric) shows a Semitic author for whom Greek was a second language, undercutting the "too good for a fisherman" argument against Petrine authorship. Other key positions: defends authentic Petrine authorship and an early date (early 50s or mid-60s), arguing the four classic pseudonymity arguments (Greek, persecution, church structure, Pauline dependence) and the "Babylon" code-word are all inconclusive, and that epistolary pseudonymity in *personal correspondence* was not an accepted, victimless convention; persecution = unofficial, local, sporadic social hostility (not state policy; the "fiery ordeal" of 4:12 = testing of faith, cf. Seneca); unity of the letter (rejects baptismal-homily/two-source theories); genre = Diaspora letter. Theology: "foreigners and resident aliens" (*parepidēmoi*/*paroikoi*) as controlling identity (literal + spiritual); suffering for the name following Christ's pattern; divine fatherhood; salvation past/present/future; inaugurated eschatology (judgment begins with God's house, 4:17); Peter's early/creative Christology — the **original NT identification of Jesus with the Isaiah 53 Suffering Servant** (2:21–25, exegeting Jesus' death in light of Isa 53, not vice versa). Cruxes: **3:18–22** = Christ's *ascension* proclamation of victory over the disobedient fallen angels of Noah's day (Dalton/Selwyn line), not a descent into hell and not (Grudem) preaching through Noah; Noah–baptism typology; **4:6** ("gospel preached to the dead") = those now dead who heard while alive, severed from 3:19, no postmortem second chance; **household code (2:18–3:7)** = differentiated culture engagement (Volf), neither Elliott's sectarian resistance nor Balch's acculturation; on wives, the suffering in view is verbal abuse/loss of standing, not domestic violence (3:7 "delicately prohibits" abuse).

Pages created: texts/1-peter (new); commentators/karen-jobes (new — first solo woman commentator); figures/peter-apostle (new — the wiki's second figure page); controversies/1-peter-authorship (new — incl. colonization theory); controversies/spirits-in-prison-1-peter-3 (new); controversies/1-peter-christ-and-culture (new — Elliott vs. Balch); concepts/christian-exile-diaspora (new).

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Green, Gene L. *Jude and 2 Peter*. BECNT. Baker Academic, 2008.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Evangelical; Wheaton College (prior theological teaching in Latin America); Ph.D. Aberdeen; advocate for Majority World / global theology. NB: **Gene L. Green ≠ [[joel-green|Joel B. Green]]** (Luke); distinct scholars. Method: grammatical-historical with intensive cultural-contextual reconstruction (Jewish apocalyptic, midrash/pesher, Epicurean theodicy, *vituperatio*, *imitatio*, secretarial composition) and a strong **sociology-of-honor** lens. Defends authenticity of both letters (minority view for 2 Peter).

**Jude**: authentic, by Jude the brother of James and Jesus (one of the "brothers of the Lord"), likely with secretarial help (answers the "good Greek" objection); **Palestinian Jewish-Christian** provenance (against Alexandria/Gunther and Syria); opponents are first-century **antinomians** (perverting grace into license; libertine; reject authority and the "glories"/angels; claim dream-revelations) — **not** second-century gnostics; date late 50s–early 60s. Defining crux: Jude **quotes 1 Enoch 1.9** (vv. 14–15) and alludes to the **Assumption/Testament of Moses** (v. 9, Michael vs. the devil over Moses' body) and the Enochic angelic fall (v. 6). Green argues Jude treats 1 Enoch as **authoritative, predictive revelation** (alongside Torah and apostolic prophecy) — against the apologetic moves (J. Charles's "even your Enoch"/opponents-held-it; the "all truth is God's truth" minimizing; the "authentic pre-flood tradition" defense) — but uses it **judiciously**, tied to canonical bedrock (Gen 6; Deut 33–34; Zech 3); commends Augustine's humble both/and. Doxology (vv. 24–25). The sole basis of ancient canonical doubt about Jude was this apocryphal use.

**2 Peter**: Green defends authentic Petrine authorship. Dismantles the major pseudonymity arguments: the Hellenistic style/difference from 1 Peter = a **different secretary** (Jerome); the **testament genre ≠ pseudepigraphy** because 2 Peter is *not* a testament (it lacks deathbed scene, burial, dreams/visions, blessings/curses — only a testamentary *section*, 1:12–15) — his key counter to Bauckham; opponents not gnostic; "early Catholicism" (Käsemann) doesn't fit; the alleged anachronisms ("the fathers," 3:4; "your apostles," 3:2; Paul's letters as Scripture, 3:16) are answerable; "**Simeon Peter**" (1:1) argues *for* authenticity. Opponents = eclectic "scoffers" (Epicurean-flavored skepticism about prophecy/providence/judgment; immutability-of-the-world; promise of freedom; libertinism) who deny the **parousia and final judgment** and are **apostates** — not gnostics, not strictly Epicureans. **2 Peter used Jude** (majority view) via *imitatio* (honorable re-use, not plagiarism), implying Jude's higher honor as a member of Jesus' family; 2 Peter drops Jude's apocryphal citations. Key theology: **"partakers of the divine nature" (1:4)** = moral transformation into God-like virtue, **not** ontological divinization or mere immortality (against the *theōsis* and Bauckham readings); inspiration of prophecy (1:20–21, "carried along by the Holy Spirit"); the delay of the parousia answered by God's word/patience and "a day is as a thousand years" (3:8); cosmic judgment by fire and **new heavens and new earth — renewal, not annihilation** (3:13; Isa 65:17), in a *linear* (non-Stoic) cosmology; accepts the difficult "the earth and the works in it will be **found**" reading in 3:10.

## 2026-06-06 ingest | Osborne, Grant R. *Revelation*. BECNT. Baker Academic, 2002.

Source type: BECNT (Baker Exegetical Commentary on the New Testament). Evangelical; Trinity Evangelical Divinity School (TEDS); Ph.D. Aberdeen under [[i-howard-marshall|I. Howard Marshall]]; author of *The Hermeneutical Spiral*; classical Arminian; historic premillennial. The wiki's first commentary on Revelation (and on a full-scale apocalypse). Method: grammatical-historical with intertextual/theological synthesis; tracing the theological threads that unify the book; "hermeneutics of humility" toward the symbols (read pictorially then referentially via first-century background, *not* a "newspaper" code); numbers symbolic (completeness). Key positions: (1) **Genre** = a blend of apocalypse + prophecy + letter; defines apocalyptic as revelatory visions guiding readers into a transcendent reality to encourage perseverance; Revelation breaks the convention by being *non-pseudonymous* and grounding victory in a *past* event (the slain Lamb). (2) **Johannine (apostolic) authorship** — defends the apostle John against the elder-John / Johannine-school / unknown / pseudonymous options; attributes the rough Greek and tonal differences from the Fourth Gospel to genre and visionary experience; cites the shared "oneness motif" Christology and *logos* title. (3) **Domitianic date (c. AD 95)** over Neronian (late 60s) — the developed imperial cult; the seven churches' situation (Laodicea's wealth, Nero *redivivus*, "synagogues of Satan"); concedes uncertainty. (4) **Unity** against redaction theories; **recapitulation** (seals/trumpets/bowls overlap, not strict chronology); interludes interrupt and interpret. (5) **Four interpretive schools** (historicist/preterist/idealist/futurist) — adopts an **eclectic** approach with the **futurist primary** (close to Beale's idealist-primary eclectic); "the present addressed through parallels with the future." (6) **Millennium (20:1–10)**: **historic premillennial** (future post-parousia earthly reign; Satan bound then released; thousand years *symbolic*; the climax of theodicy proving total depravity and the necessity of eternal judgment) — but holds it *irenically*, affirming amil and postmil as viable. (7) **The beast** = the final Antichrist / final unholy Rome, a Nero-like figure; the false trinity as Satan's "great imitation"; 666 symbolic (gematria for Nero Caesar the best-known proposal). (8) **Use of the OT**: no formal quotations but more allusions than any NT book (Isaiah, Daniel, Ezekiel, Zechariah, Psalms — not Daniel alone); typological *transformation* of OT contexts. Theology: **sovereignty of God** (the throne; the divine passive *edothē*); the **futility of Satan** (deception, not power; defeated at the cross); **Christology — the Lamb** (slain Lamb + conquering Ram; oneness motif = Christ's deity); the **sevenfold Spirit**; cosmic war, theodicy and *lex talionis*; a genuine **mission** theme (the "eternal gospel," two tracks for the nations); **perseverance/conquering** (eschatology grounds ethics). New Jerusalem (21:9–22:5) as the **Holy of Holies** ("no temple… for its temple is the Lord God and the Lamb," 21:22) and the **final Eden** (22:1–5).

Pages created: texts/revelation (new); commentators/grant-osborne (new); figures/john-apostle (new — the wiki's fourth figure page; completes the three Jerusalem "pillars": Peter, James, John); controversies/revelation-interpretive-schools (new); controversies/revelation-millennium (new); controversies/revelation-date-authorship (new); concepts/lamb-of-god (new).

- **France, Matthew**: Added emphasis on pericope-as-wholes treatment before verse detail; deliberate organization around overall narrative flow ("woods before trees"); structure as reader response rather than recovered authorial blueprint.
- **France, Mark**: Expanded "Drama in Three Acts" with oral-performance features (εὐθύς, parataxis, historic present, vivid aural details); prologue function (privileged reader knowledge vs. characters); explanatory discourses as narrative pauses; christology "in the story"; Act Two as sustained disciple re-education framed by blind-healings.
- **Green, Luke**: Added detailed literary blocks (birth narrative structures with paired songs and social location; Galilean ministry anchored on Nazareth sermon/Jubilee; Travel Narrative 9:51–19:48 as distinctive Lukan creation; Jerusalem as goal/center); theocentric historiography (God's δεῖ and Spirit as plot drivers); "narration is proclamation"; discourse analysis + social-scientific lens (honor-shame, purity, kinship, reversal of the lowly); Luke-Acts unity reinforced with narrative completeness argument.
- **Michaels, John**: Enhanced structure with "preamble" (1:1–5) vs. traditional prologue distinction; narrative proper opening at 1:6 with John's testimony; public ministry (4–12) framed as extended cosmic trial/judgment of Jesus by authorities and of the world by Jesus; testimony (martyria) chain as governing narrative framework (Father → Son → Spirit/Advocate → Beloved Disciple → Gospel → reader); five-part outline anchored on non-narrative theological passages.
- **Bock, Acts**: Added genre classification as ancient historical monograph (not classic Greco-Roman "acts" of a single hero); God as the true central character (plan, activity, orchestration), with Spirit as enabling power sent by the risen Jesus; selectivity of presentation (not every community or figure treated); "we passages" as eyewitness support; speeches as authentic summaries fitting settings and speakers; four dominant interlocking issues (Gentile inclusion without Torah, Jewish non-response yet remnant, Jesus' ongoing exalted lordship, faithful witnesses as models).

  - [[simeon|Simeon]] — Full from Green (righteous/devout, Nunc Dimittis, universal light + division oracle, sets Gospel trajectory).
  - [[zechariah-priest|Zechariah (Priest)]] — Full from Green (temple choreography, doubt/silencing vs. restoration, Benedictus as covenant theology, theocentric parallel to Israel's story).
- [[theophilus|Theophilus]] — Full from Green (real high-status patron/"most excellent," needs "certainty" of orderly account, representative audience) + Bock (unity of Luke-Acts, historical monograph purpose).
- [[john-apostle|John the Apostle (Son of Zebedee)]] — Enhanced with Michaels (testimony framework, Beloved Disciple possibilities, preamble structure, epilogue addressing community after his death) + Acts pillar references (Bock).
- [[james-the-just|James the Just]] — Enhanced with Gospels (family unbelief then resurrection appearance) + Acts leadership/Council (Bock) + kinship and Jewish-Christian portrait.
- [[jude-brother-of-jesus|Jude (the Brother of Jesus)]] — Enhanced with Gospels (family unbelief, *desposynoi*) + Acts 1:14 (upper room) + Green's Palestinian Jewish-Christian provenance, *imitatio*, and authoritative use of 1 Enoch.
- [[paul-apostle|Paul the Apostle]] — Enhanced with Bock (conversion as direct Christophany, God as central actor in Acts, "we" passages, speeches as summaries, four dominant issues, companions as co-workers, historical monograph selectivity).
- Companions (enhanced with Bock/Acts material on team mission, God-centered orchestration, specific roles):
  - [[barnabas|Barnabas]] — Generosity, advocate for Paul, first journey partner, model of generosity and bridge-builder; honest split over Mark as part of larger plan.
  - [[silas|Silas (Silvanus)]] — Jerusalem prophet/co-worker on second journey; co-sender of Thessalonian letters; link between Jerusalem and Gentile mission.
  - [[timothy|Timothy]] — Trusted delegate on second journey; co-sender; risky service; recipient of Pastorals (Yarbrough cross-reference for experience).
  - [[titus|Titus]] — Uncircumcised Greek test case (Gal. 2); emissary to Corinth; delegate to Crete (inclusion embodied).

Index.md updated with expanded NT figures section, noting batch contributions.

All pages now include explicit hermeneutical contributions from the five sources, multi-tradition reception where applicable, and cross-links. This completes the figures extraction/upgrade requirement for the NT re-ingest batch (R3). Narrative pages were handled in the prior sub-ingest.

Next: remaining NT sources (Paulines, General Epistles, Hebrews, Revelation) or full verification.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Processing the rest of the NT commentaries for narratives and figures (excluding Romans, 1 Peter, Jude/2 Peter, Revelation)

Source type: The remaining NT sources after the core Gospels+Acts batch and the explicitly skipped ones (Romans, 1 Peter, Jude/2 Peter, Revelation). Narratives first (enhance or create text pages), then figures (new or enhanced pages per CLAUDE.md schema).

**Narratives (text pages)**:
- New text pages created per schema (no prior pages existed):
  - [[philemon|Letter to Philemon]] — Full narrative from McKnight (NICNT): three escalating appeals for Onesimus (presentation, partnership, presence); rhetorical structure; major themes of reconciliation, brotherhood in Christ ("more than a slave, a beloved brother"), providence ("perhaps this is why he was parted"), and direct application to modern slaveries/racism. Hermeneutical: social realities of slavery/patronage + pastoral urgency.
  - [[philippians|Letter to the Philippians]] — Full narrative from Hansen (PNTC): rhetorical structure centered on 1:27 ("live worthy of the gospel"); Christ hymn (2:6-11) as paradigm; partnership (κοινωνία); joy in suffering; warning against Judaizers/libertines; eschatological citizenship (3:20). Major themes: gospel partnership, suffering as advance of the gospel, unity/humility modeled on Christ, joy.
- For other sources in this batch (1 Cor, 2 Cor, Gal, Eph, Col, 1-2 Thess, 1-2 Tim/Titus, James, 1-3 John, Hebrews): Corresponding text pages already had substantial narrative content from prior ingests of these exact sources (e.g., 1 Cor with Ciampa-Rosner Jewish-moral framework and OT templates; 2 Cor with Seifrid theologia crucis and apostolic legitimation; Gal with Moo's cross/new age structure and Abraham argument; Hebrews with Cockerill's rhetorical/typological sermon shape; James with Moo's wisdom structure; 1-3 John with Marshall/Kruse on secessionist crisis and testimony). Processing confirmed and noted the existing rich narrative sections; no major new additions required beyond the new Philemon/Philippians pages.

**Figures (new and enhanced)**:
- New figures created (full schema, substantial treatment in the sources):
  - [[onesimus|Onesimus]] — Full from McKnight (Philemon): runaway slave; "my child" to Paul; central "test case" for gospel brotherhood transcending slavery; three appeals; modern application to slaveries. Sections: bio, appearances (Phlm + Col 4:9), tradition-specific (early church, abolitionist use, contemporary), theological/narrative (reconciliation with teeth, presentation rhetoric), historicity (offense, manumission debate, providence), influence (McKnight's "emancipation" of the letter; pastoral model of intercession).
  - [[epaphroditus|Epaphroditus]] — Full from Hansen (Philippians): Philippian messenger/minister; nearly died; "fellow worker, fellow soldier, your messenger and minister"; model of risky service and gospel partnership. Sections: bio, appearances (Phil 2:25-30; 4:18), tradition-specific, theological/narrative (liturgy as service, risk for the work of Christ, joyful welcome), historicity (illness, "lacking in your service"), influence (Hansen on concrete illustration of 1:27 and partnership themes).
- Enhanced existing figures with material from this batch's sources (adding to or filling sections per schema, with specific commentator insights):
  - [[paul-apostle|Paul the Apostle]] — Added from 2 Cor (Seifrid: theologia crucis as hermeneutical key; apostle as "Christian existence in large-screen display"; new covenant ministry; reconciliation and great exchange; legitimation through weakness); from Gal (Moo: autobiography as defense of the gospel; truth of the gospel defined in 2:15-21); from Eph/Col (Thielman/McKnight: imprisonment context, cosmic Christology, household codes); from Pastorals (Yarbrough: servant/apostle self-understanding, global Christianity hermeneutic, defense of miracles); from 1 Cor (Ciampa-Rosner: social values thesis, OT templates); from Thess (Weima: epistolary-literary method, anti-imperial); from James (Moo cross: James/Paul justification tension); from 1-3 John (Marshall/Kruse cross: Johannine vs. Pauline); from Hebrews (Cockerill cross: high priestly Christology).
  - [[melchizedek|Melchizedek]] — Enhanced from Cockerill (Hebrews): greater than Levi (7:1-10); priest "in the likeness of Melchizedek" displaces Aaron (7:11-25); "exactly the kind of priest we need" (7:26-28); rhetorical/typological use in the sermon's structure; anti-neo-Platonic apocalyptic worldview.
  - [[john-apostle|John the Apostle]] — Enhanced from Marshall (Epistles of John) and Kruse (Letters of John): secessionist crisis (proto-Docetic/Cerinthian); mirror reading; "elder" title; scenario-based reconstruction; propitiation emphasis; rhetorical analysis; 24 excursuses in Kruse.
  - [[james-the-just|James the Just]] — Enhanced from Moo (James): spiritual wholeness as organizing concern (*teleios* vs. *dipsychos*); traditional authorship + early date; two-senses of *dikaioō* resolution with Paul; covenantal nomism qualified; anointing as consecration symbol; practical "sermon/homily in letter form."
  - Other enhancements (Paul companions, Timothy/Titus already strong from prior but cross-updated; minor from these sources like Tychicus in Eph/Col, etc.).

- New figures: onesimus.md, epaphroditus.md (added to index under NT figures).

## 2026-06-07 ingest | OT start: Pentateuch narratives and figures (Genesis group — Hamilton NICOT vols. 1–2)

Source type: Modern evangelical commentary (NICOT series). Two volumes covering the entire book of Genesis. Primary focus on narrative structure via the *tôlĕdôt* formula, primeval history (degeneration), patriarchal stories (election and promise), covenant theology, providence, and engagement with source criticism/DH while defending canonical and evangelical readings.

**Narratives (text page enhancement)**:
- Enhanced [[genesis|Genesis]] (wiki/texts/genesis.md):
  - Expanded Structure section with Hamilton's detailed analysis of the ten *tôlĕdôt* formulae (five narrative "this is the story of X," five genealogical headings). Hamilton defends 2:4a as superscript (not subscript to 1:1–2:3) on grammatical, versional (LXX), and contextual grounds; discusses colophons vs. titles and interacts with Wiseman-Harrison tablet theory (prefers standard introductory rubric view). Notes unique lack of personal name in first *tôlĕdôt* (heavens and earth) and polarity with creation account (divine image vs. earthly creatureliness). Emphasizes geographical poles (Babylonia–Canaan–Egypt) and movement from universal failure (chs. 1–11) to patriarchal promise (chs. 12–50).
  - Expanded Major Themes with Hamilton-specific narrative emphases: primeval *tôlĕdôt* arc of degeneration answered by election; dual covenant ratifications (Gen 15/17) as one unconditional covenant; younger-son reversals as election by grace; Joseph story as climax resolving family tensions via hidden providence ("God intended it for good," 50:20). Added note on absence of theogony (God has no *rē’šîṯ*) and interplay of vertical/horizontal genealogies.
  - Updated Sources Ingested and Key Commentators with Hamilton's distinctive contributions: comprehensive evangelical treatment; philological/grammatical detail (e.g., *bĕrēʾšît*); defense of traditional readings (absolute "in the beginning," historicity within ANE context) while engaging scholarship; treatment of pericopes as literary wholes; complementary creation accounts; *tôlĕdôt* as book's "lines of demarcation."

**Figures (enhancement)**:
- Enhanced [[abraham|Abraham]] (wiki/figures/abraham.md):
  - Expanded Biographical Overview with Hamilton's division of Abraham story (Gen 11:27–25:11, the sixth *tôlĕdôt*): call as divine initiative from pagan Ur, unconditional promises despite failure (Hagar, wife-sister deceptions), dual covenants, Akedah (Gen 22) as climactic faith test (obedience "here I am," ram as substitution/providence), name change, death at 175. Added Hamilton on Abraham as "righteous" by faith (15:6) yet growing in trust (e.g., the laughter in 17:17 and Sarah's in 18:12).
  - Expanded Primary Source Appearances with Hamilton's pericopes: call/sojourns (12:1-9), deceptions (12:10–13:18), covenant/Hagar (14–16), renewal/circumcision (17), Sodom/Isaac birth (18–21), Akedah (22), wife for Isaac and death (23–25). Cross-referenced existing NT appearances.
  - Expanded Tradition-Specific Reception (Jewish, Christian evangelical per Hamilton/NT, Islamic, Samaritan, critical). Added Philo allegory, patristic typology, Reformation justification emphasis, modern canonical/form-critical.
  - Expanded Theological and Narrative Significance with Hamilton: Abraham as answer to primeval crisis (Gen 1–11); structures the book via *tôlĕdôt* of Terah; models election by grace, faith as trust (15:6), covenant as divine commitment, sojourning tension. Younger-son reversals, deceptions, Akedah highlight sovereignty over frailty. Universal scope (blessing to nations).
  - Expanded Historicity and Interpretive Controversies with Hamilton: Ur/migration (defends substantial historicity in 2nd-millennium context); covenant(s) (one in stages vs. two; canonical unity over source criticism); Akedah (supreme test, substitution typology, ethical issues); wife-sister stories (distinct episodes, divine protection); Melchizedek (historical or insertion? typology).
  - Expanded Influence on Commentary Traditions with Hamilton's specific contributions (pivotal figure resolving primeval failure; philological/theological exegesis; evangelical readings vs. source criticism; ANE engagement) plus cross-tradition notes (Philo/Origen allegory vs. literal-historical).

**Bookkeeping**:
- Index.md not yet updated for new OT figures (Abraham already listed; no new pages created).
- Log.md appended with this entry (Pentateuch/Genesis start; narratives first then figures per user direction and prior NT pattern).
- sources_ingested and last_updated refreshed on genesis.md (now reflects Hamilton emphasis) and abraham.md.
- No new controversies/concepts promoted; existing links preserved/enhanced (e.g., toledot, covenant, Akedah, creation-ex-nihilo, flood-universality, sons-of-god, genesis-creation-accounts, genesis-ancient-near-east).
- Verification pending full OT batch (will run lint/check after more books).

This begins the OT re-ingest per CLAUDE.md (identify source — evangelical OT commentary; discuss via extraction; write/update text pages first with narrative, then figure pages for named figures with substantial treatment like Abraham; full schema; hermeneutical tracking; index/log updates). Hamilton provides rich evangelical narrative architecture and patriarchal portraits (Christian reception). Next: continue Pentateuch (Exodus via Bruckner, etc.) or move to figures for other patriarchs (Isaac/Jacob/Joseph if substantial in Hamilton) or next book group (e.g., Exodus narrative + Moses figure).

## 2026-06-07 ingest | OT Pentateuch (Exodus — Bruckner UBCS)

Source type: Evangelical "believing criticism" commentary (UBCS series). James K. Bruckner on Exodus. Moderate on redaction (6th-cent. crisis context), strong on final-form literary structure (chiastic tabernacle-crisis-tabernacle), creational theology, and canonical reading. Primary narrative focus: deliverance from Egypt, progressive revelation of YHWH's name/character, covenant at Sinai, golden calf crisis as hinge (forgiveness + renewed presence), tabernacle as God's dwelling with sinners.

**Narratives (text page confirmation/enhancement)**:
- [[exodus|Exodus]] (wiki/texts/exodus.md) already had substantial Bruckner-based content (6-section structure with chiastic center in golden calf crisis; major themes of divine presence/name revelation/exodus as liberation paradigm/law as freedom/tabernacle as new creation/God's character in crisis; key cruxes like divine name, hardening of heart, Passover logic, 600k men, tabernacle historicity, Exod 34:6-7). Added/confirmed Bruckner's "believing criticism" method (Childs canonical + Fretheim creational; moderate source engagement; final form in Babylonian crisis); emphasis on plagues as "signs", Moses as mediator/intercessor, golden calf as theological center revealing the 13 attributes (most-quoted OT text on God's mercy/justice); engagement with liberation theology (paradigm + its limits re: Canaanites) and modern applications. Sources section updated with Bruckner's distinctive contributions.

**Figures (enhancement)**:
- Enhanced [[moses|Moses]] (wiki/figures/moses.md):
  - Expanded Biographical Overview with Bruckner's Exodus focus: birth/oppression, burning bush call (reluctance, signs, "I AM"), plagues/exodus (mediator of signs, Passover), wilderness to Sinai (provision, Jethro), theophany/law (Decalogue + Book of the Covenant), golden calf intercession (Exod 32-34: "if you will forgive... blot me out"; "show me your glory"), tabernacle oversight (instructions + building). Bruckner: Moses embodies God's creational/redemptive purposes; progressive name/character revelation through him; reluctant → empowered intercessor.
  - Expanded Primary Source Appearances: Detailed Exodus pericopes per Bruckner (6 sections: exit, journey, law/covenant, instructions, crisis/forgiveness/name, building/presence); cross to Lev/Num/Deut (law, census, rebellions, death); existing NT (Heb 3/11, Acts 7, 2 Cor 3, Jude 9).
  - Expanded Tradition-Specific Reception: Jewish (Moshe Rabbenu, greatest prophet, 613 mitzvot, face-to-face with God); Christian (type of Christ/deliverer/mediator; faithful servant in Heb 3; intercessor prefiguring Jesus; liberation paradigm); Islamic (Musa prophet, Tawrat, confronts Fir'aun); Samaritan (supreme prophet, Gerizim); critical (historical Moses debated, sources J/E/P, form-critical etiological). Bruckner: believing criticism lens on final form.
  - Expanded Theological and Narrative Significance: Moses as human face of exodus paradigm (slavery → covenant people → God's dwelling/tabernacle); mediator of progressive name revelation (I AM → compassionate/gracious/slow to anger/abounding in ḥesed → "will by no means clear the guilty"); typological (servant vs. Son in Heb; law vs. grace); canonical (Torah through Moses; points to greater prophet Deut 18). Bruckner: answers "Will Yahweh go with them?" via Moses' intercession + glory in tabernacle (Exod 40).
  - Expanded Historicity and Interpretive Controversies: Moses' historicity (traditional 13th cent. vs. critical composite; no direct extra-biblical for exodus); hardening of heart (Pharaoh/God/both; Bruckner: intensification of persistent rebellion for name proclamation); numbers (600k as clans/hyperbole); tabernacle (P source? ancient roots vs. post-exilic fiction; Bruckner affirms theological reality); golden calf (polemic vs. Jeroboam?); death/authorship (Deut 34).
  - Expanded Influence on Commentary Traditions: Bruckner (UBCS): believing criticism, creational/canonical (Childs/Fretheim); Moses as empowered leader model; plagues as signs; golden calf crisis as hinge for 34:6-7 (forgiveness + consequences); engages liberation theology limits. Cockerill (Heb): faithfulness honored yet surpassed. Jewish midrash; patristic typology (Moses/Christ); Reformation (law/gospel); modern liberation (Moses revolutionary) + canonical/creational.
- Created [[miriam|Miriam]] (wiki/figures/miriam.md) — full schema from Bruckner (Exodus song leadership) and Cole (Numbers rebellion, leprosy as speech ethics lesson). Substantial treatment: prophetess, leader of women, sibling challenge to Moses' authority, death at Kadesh. Sections: bio (sister who saved Moses, song at sea, Num 12 leprosy), appearances (Exod 2/15, Num 12/20, Deut 24, Mic 6, 1 Chron 6), tradition-specific (Jewish 7 prophetesses + well tradition; Christian women's prophecy model + cautionary tale; Islamic parallels; critical layers), significance (female prophetic agency, limits of authority, humility before God's mediator), historicity (etiology of slander/leprosy; sibling triad), influence (midrash, feminist reclamation, speech ethics in leadership).

**Bookkeeping**:
- Log.md appended (Exodus narrative + Moses/Miriam figures per Bruckner/Cole).
- sources_ingested/last_updated refreshed (exodus.md, moses.md, new miriam.md).
- New figure miriam.md; index.md updated (added to NT/cross-tradition figures).
- Links enhanced (divine-name, covenant, tabernacle, passover, genesis, hebrews, miriam cross to aaron/moses).
- Verification pending full Pentateuch (run after Deuteronomy).

This continues systematic OT re-ingest (Pentateuch: Genesis/Exodus done; now Leviticus/Numbers). Narratives first (texts enhanced with commentator-specific structure/themes), then figures (Moses enhanced; Miriam new full page). Per CLAUDE.md (full schema, hermeneutical tracking via "believing criticism"/creational, log append, etc.). Next: Leviticus (Wenham) + Aaron figure (if not done), then Deuteronomy (Craigie/Arnold) + Joshua/Caleb figures, etc.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Woudstra, Marten H. *Joshua*. NICOT. Eerdmans, 1981.

Source type: NICOT (New International Commentary on the Old Testament). Reformed (Christian Reformed), Calvin Seminary; also served on NIV translation committee. Method: grammatico-historical with strong attention to literary structure, design, and unity of composition (discovered during writing); "prophetical" historiography read theocentrically (history from God's perspective, dynamic movement to a goal, "already/not yet" rather than primarily example-model); "listening to the narrator" and ultimately to the "great Someone Else"; covenant as occasion for preserving the memory of YHWH's acts.

**Narratives (text page enhancement)**:
- [[joshua|Joshua]] (wiki/texts/joshua.md) already contained a strong Woudstra-derived narrative (3-part structure: Conquered 1–12 / Distributed 13–22 / Kept in Covenant Obedience 23–24; symmetry with ch. 1 + 23–24 hortatory frames and maʿal framing of Achan ch. 7 / altar ch. 22; theocentric historiography as controlling principle; herem as judgment per Gen 15:16; already/not yet of possession 21:43–45 vs 13:1; land as YHWH's naḥalâ; covenant obedience condition; detailed key episodes Rahab confession 2:11 + scarlet cord, Jordan crossing as Moses parallel with ark leading, Jericho as liturgical not military, Achan/Ai as central covenant lesson, Gibeonite deception honored because sworn in YHWH's name, Shechem renewal ch. 24 with "you cannot serve the LORD, for he is a holy God"; composition: basic unity vs Hexateuch/DH, early post-Joshua date, vivid eyewitness-quality; reception Jewish, patristic Origen allegory (Woudstra critiques excess while affirming Heb 4 typology), liberation theology tension (Woudstra hostile to example-method), Woudstra rejects modern territorial land claims; Joshua-Jesus typology Heb 4:8 "rest"; sources section). 
- Enhancement added Woudstra-specifics from preface/intro and commentary: explicit "prophetical" historiography ("the great Someone Else"; history en route to goal, already/not yet); detailed unity-of-composition frames (maʿal Achan/altar; Anakim fear-to-expulsion; Caleb/Joshua inheritance bracketing allotment; burials at end as fulfillment contrast to Gen 23; episodic Hebrew narrative with prolepsis and thematic shaping rather than "rough spots" proving composite); cultus emphasis (circumcision/Passover 5:1–12, Ebal/Gerizim 8:30–35, altar controversy ch. 22, lots at Shiloh with tent/Eleazar 18–19); God as Lord of all the earth recognized by Rahab (2:11; 3:11); Achan treatment designed to show "the holy land must be inhabited by holy people" (God's anger + human responsibility); "be strong and courageous" as recurring keynote; covenant occasion for the book (keep memory alive for trust/obedience; eschatological extension Ps 37/Matt 5); God gives land yet Israel acts as moral agent; hardens hearts yet notes Canaanite resolve; spares Rahab for kindness (6:17) and punishes Achan's house. Hermeneutical tracking: theocentric vs anthropocentric; structure/design serves total message; exegesis as humble reception of meaning with contemporary relevance. YAML sources_ingested bumped (1→2), last_updated refreshed.

**Figures (enhancement + new full-schema pages)**:

land given yet claimed/fought for; frames Judah allotment (14:6–15 faith opening vs 15:63 failure closing) and brackets distribution with Joshua's inheritance (19:49–50) — the two faithful spies open and close; Hebron/Anakim dating/traditions; Achsah doublets etiological?; promise vs conditioned faithfulness; longevity stylized vs literal. **Influence on Commentary Traditions**: Woudstra et al. on structural design (Caleb materials illustrate book's frames and theme of faith realizing the impossible); **Figures (new full-schema pages created)**:
- Created [[deborah|Deborah]] (wiki/figures/deborah.md): Full schema. Prophet/judge under palm (Ephraim), summons Barak, "if you go I go" condition transfers glory to woman; Jael's tent-peg act; Song of Deborah (oldest Hebrew poetry) celebrates willing tribes, indicts absent (Reuben etc.), ends with Sisera's mother at window rationalizing. **Biographical**: wife of Lappidoth, "mother in Israel." **Tradition**: Jewish (one of 7 prophetesses); Christian (Spirit-empowered female leadership, Heb 11 context, feminist recovery of Jael); critical (early poem, etiological lament). **Significance**: YHWH as true actor; reversal/surprise (woman delivers); willing hearts honored; tribal indictment; motherly protective leadership vs oppressive kings. Links to judges, nazarite (contrast Samson), hebrews.
- Created [[gideon|Gideon]] (Jerubbaal) (wiki/figures/gideon.md): Full schema. Threshes in winepress; angel "mighty man of valor"; fleece signs; destroys father's Baal altar by night; Spirit clothes him; army reduced 32k→300 (lapping vs kneeling); torches/jars/trumpets rout Midian; pursues, threshes Succoth/Penuel elders; executes Zebah/Zalmunna; refuses kingship ("LORD will rule") but makes gold ephod that becomes snare/idol; 70 sons + Abimelech by concubine; dies old, immediate apostasy follows. **Turning point**: begins Moses-like reluctant/dependent, ends compromised like Aaron; Abimelech (name "my father is king") kills 70 brothers on one stone, buys kingship, dies by millstone (grotesque parody). **Significance**: hinge of book; after him "rest" never again said; illustrates power through weakness + speed of Canaanization/syncretism. Links to judges, abimelech, samson, hebrews.
- Created [[jephthah|Jephthah]] (wiki/figures/jephthah.md): Full schema. Son of prostitute, driven out, gathers "worthless fellows" in Tob; elders beg him back, he negotiates hard ("if I win I stay as head"); long diplomatic history-law message to Ammon king (ignored); vow before battle ("whatever comes out... I will offer as burnt offering"); daughter with tambourines comes first; she accepts, requests 2 months bewail virginity on mountains; "he did with her according to his vow"; annual 4-day lament by daughters of Israel. Later Shibboleth slaughter of 42k Ephraimites at Jordan fords. Judges 6 years. **Tradition**: Jewish (vow crux — literal sacrifice vs perpetual virginity; warning against rash oaths); Christian (tragic bargaining with God; Heb 11 lists him anyway; daughter's agency recovered); critical (diplomatic speech later insertion; Greek parallels). **Significance**: treats YHWH as negotiable power like everything else; capable deliverer compromised by vow; "LORD the Judge decide" (11:27); prepares epilogues moral chaos. Links to judges, jephthah-vow controversy, covenant, hebrews.
- Created [[samson|Samson]] (wiki/figures/samson.md): Full schema. Birth to barren mother announced by messenger (Nazirite from womb, no razor/wine/unclean; begin to save from Philistines); riddles, lion honey (vow violation?), foxes/torches, jawbone 1000, Delilah (7 locks shaved while sleeping), eyes gouged, Gaza mill, final Dagon temple pillars collapse (kills more in death than life). Buried in father's tomb. **Webb's key**: "Samson's personal story *is* Israel's national story" — set apart (Nazirite/Israel) yet chases foreign (women/gods); wants to be "as other men/nations"; birth narrative raises great deliverer expectation; life delivers personal vendettas; death is greatest victory + most complete humiliation (blind broken man). **Significance**: nadir of downward spiral; 20 years judge; "made strong out of weakness" (Heb 11); grace through compromised vessel. Links to judges, nazarite, deborah/gideon/jephthah, hebrews, samuel (contrast).

Source type: NICOT (recent volume). Ethnically Chinese Australian (Hong Kong-born, Sydney-raised); PhD on social identity and ethics in Ruth (Sydney University, Jewish supervisors); cross-cultural mission experience in Southeast Asia; medical doctor part-time; Reformed-leaning nondenominational background with sensitivity to honor-shame, fear-power, postcolonial, and missional readings. Method: close literary reading + social-scientific (honor-shame, social identity) + canonical/theological with strong practical/missional application; *ḥesed* as ethical and theological center.

**Narratives (text page enhancement)**:
- [[ruth|Ruth]] (wiki/texts/ruth.md) already had rich content (canonical positions table and Lau quote on not privileging one; 4-act chiasm with double pivot at "Boaz is kinsman-redeemer" and threshing-floor scheme; themes of emptiness→fullness, *ḥesed*, hidden providence, Davidic prehistory). Enhancement added Lau-specifics: honor-shame and social-identity framework from his background and context (minority belonging, cross-cultural mission, "sandwich generation," medical listening); *ḥesed* as covenant loyalty beyond legal obligation, often at cost to social standing; missional reading (Ruth the outsider embodies inclusion of nations in Davidic line; boundary-crossing for the other); practical/contextual emphasis (theory and praxis not separated; addresses real crises of belonging/provision/identity); agency of the women (Naomi strategist, Ruth bold initiator, townswomen chorus); quiet providence through ordinary human *ḥesed* ( "it happened" 2:3); kinsman-redeemer as human agent of divine restoration; genealogy as "grand narrative" claim running through the marginalized/foreigner. Hermeneutical tracking: honor-shame + missional + canonical (within evangelical NICOT convictions of Scripture as God-breathed and useful). YAML sources 1→2, last_updated refreshed.

**Figures (new full-schema pages)**:
- Created [[ruth|Ruth]] (wiki/figures/ruth.md): Full schema. Moabite widow of Mahlon; clings to Naomi with "your God my God" vow (1:16–17); gleans in Boaz's field; threshing-floor initiative ("spread your wings... you are a redeemer" 3:9); marries Boaz; bears Obed (great-grandmother of David); Matt 1:5. **Tradition**: Jewish (paradigmatic convert/giyoret, Shavuot reading); Christian (Gentile inclusion, *ḥesed* ethics, kinsman-redeemer typology of Christ, grace in genealogy with Tamar/Rahab/Bathsheba); critical (protest vs. exclusion or Davidic apology; honor-shame boundary-crossing). **Significance**: *ḥesed* embodied (clings, risks, exceeds first loyalty); hidden providence ("it happened"); answers Judges emptiness with fullness; Moabite in Davidic line signals nations blessed. Links to ruth text, naomi, boaz, hesed, kinsman-redeemer, david, matthew, judges, ezra-nehemiah (inclusion tension).
- Created [[naomi|Naomi]] (wiki/figures/naomi.md): Full schema. Loses husband + sons in Moab; returns "empty" ("call me Mara... LORD has brought me back empty" 1:20–21); urges daughters-in-law to turn back (Orpah leaves, Ruth clings); accepts gleaning plan; initiates threshing-floor scheme for Ruth's "rest"; receives Obed, nurses him; women say Ruth "more to you than seven sons," "restorer of life." **Tradition**: Jewish (mother-in-law who receives ultimate *ḥesed* from foreign daughter-in-law; lament-to-reversal paradigm); Christian (honest lament + faith in action; restored by outsider's loyalty; surrogate-mother dynamics); critical/feminist (complex agency — despair to strategy; female community solidarity). **Significance**: arc from full→empty→restored; honest lament not rebuked; *ḥesed* chain restores name/land/future/child in old age; answers Judges crisis at intimate level. Links to ruth text, ruth, boaz, hesed, judges, lamentations.
- Created [[boaz|Boaz]] (wiki/figures/boaz.md): Full schema. "Worthy man" (*gibbôr ḥayil*) of Elimelech's clan; arrives "The LORD be with you"; notices Ruth, protects ("do not touch"), provides generously (roasted grain, extra sheaves, water); praises her *ḥesed* greater than first and her worth ("all my fellow townsmen know you are a worthy woman" 3:11); at gate publicly redeems land + Ruth "to perpetuate the name of the dead" before elders/witnesses; nearer kinsman declines; marries Ruth; Obed born; genealogy Salmon→Boaz→Obed→Jesse→David. **Tradition**: Jewish (model *gibbôr ḥayil* of *ḥesed*, just gate procedure, communal blessing like Rachel/Leah/Perez); Christian (kinsman-redeemer typology of Christ; godly manhood, protection of vulnerable, public justice); critical/social-scientific (status used to elevate rather than exploit; honor restoration for outsider). **Significance**: human agent of divine restoration; goes beyond minimum law; willing to "impair" for name/living vs. nearer kinsman's self-interest; makes possible Davidic line through Moabite. Links to ruth text, ruth, naomi, hesed, kinsman-redeemer, david, matthew, judges.
