XI–XII (Hecker and Wolski) for additional material. The distinction between the main Torah commentary and the earlier concealed midrashic layer is now explicitly tracked in the wiki.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Rudolph, Kurt. *Gnosis: The Nature and History of Gnosticism* (HarperSanFrancisco, 1987; German orig. 1977, rev. 1980; trans. ed. Robert McLachlan Wilson).

Source type: major scholarly monograph / synthesis (phenomenological + historical-critical) on Gnosticism as a late antique religion. Author: Kurt Rudolph (East German scholar of religion, specialist in Gnosticism, Mandaeism, and comparative late antique religions). The book remains the standard English-language reference work drawing on both heresiological sources and the full implications of the Nag Hammadi discoveries.

**Key contributions** (drawn from the book's structure and Rudolph's framing):
(1) **Phenomenological "nature" of Gnosis as a coherent religion**: Systematic description of the main features of Gnostic ideology and mythology — radical dualism (spirit vs. matter; alien transcendent God vs. this world); cosmology/cosmogony (Pleroma of aeons in syzygies; Sophia's fall/passion producing deficiency and the Demiurge/Yaldabaoth as inferior, ignorant creator often identified with the OT God; archons as cosmic powers); anthropology (composite human with trapped pneumatic spark from the higher realm); soteriology of *gnosis* (revealed esoteric knowledge as awakening, disclosure of origin and structure, and enablement of the soul's ascent through the archons back to the Pleroma); community, cult, and ethics (organized groups, sacraments including Valentinian "bridal chamber," ascetic and libertine poles). Gnosis is "knowledge that saves" because it effects liberation from the Demiurge's dominion.
(2) **Nag Hammadi as revolutionary primary sources**: The 1945 discovery (13 codices, 52+ Coptic texts near Nag Hammadi, likely buried by Pachomian monks c. 367 CE after Athanasius' canon list) transforms the field from reliance on hostile secondary (heresiological) accounts to direct Gnostic voices. Confirms basic reliability of Irenaeus on Valentinianism while revealing greater diversity, sophistication, and self-understanding. Rudolph devotes significant attention to its significance for reconstruction.
(3) **Historical development and "great systems"**: Survey of the "problem of origins" (multiple presuppositions: Jewish apocalyptic/Genesis exegesis, Hellenistic Platonism and mysteries, Iranian dualism, Christian motifs reinterpreted — no monocausal solution). Early schools (Simonians, Basilideans, Ophites); the 2nd-c. "great systems" with Valentinianism as the most elaborate (Pleroma of 30 aeons, sophisticated exegesis, tripartite anthropology); Manichaeism treated as Gnosis "in a new, world-historical form" (Mani's systematization, missionary religion with canon and church structure, spread across Eurasia); Mandaeans as the only surviving Gnostic community (living empirical witness to ancient structures, repeated baptisms, mythology).
(4) **Social, cultic, and ethical dimensions often underemphasized**: Full treatment of community organization, sacraments, and ethics (not purely "myth for intellectuals"). Valentinian sacramental life and the tension between world-rejecting asceticism and other interpretations receive attention.
(5) **Balanced, non-polemical portrait**: Gnostics are presented as participants in a serious, syncretistic but structurally coherent religious movement of late antiquity with their own claim to possess the true gnosis of the alien God. Not romanticized as "suppressed true Christianity" nor reduced to "corrupters" or "verbal Christianizing of paganism." The epilogue surveys later consequences and the modern scholarly recovery.

**Contradictions / tensions flagged (not flattened)**: Rudolph's phenomenological + multi-root historical approach (Gnosis as distinct *spätantike Religion* with structural independence, possible pre-/para-Christian Jewish strata especially in Sethianism, and primary Nag Hammadi voices) stands in productive tension with the wiki's existing early-Christianity layers: Stark's quantitative argument (Gnosticism as Christian heresy; appears where Christianity is established; no independent synagogue effect when Christianization controlled — supporting Harnack); Kruger and patristic sources (Gnostics as primary "anathematized" foil; "the few" per their own admission; diversity real but orthodoxy early and identifiable via rule of faith and apostolic networks); Wolfson (Gnosticism as "verbal Christianizing of paganism" — syncretisms into tiered systems classified by number of beings, not true philosophy, logical outcome of difficulties in catholic positions on Trinity/Incarnation). Rudolph does not deny Christian intersection or use of Christian scriptures/language (esp. Valentinians) but gives equal weight to the myth's internal coherence, the Demiurge as a genuine religious problem (not mere polemic), and the social/ethical reality of Gnostic communities. The two framings (heresy/foil vs. independent religious phenomenon) are not fully resolved by the sources; Rudolph supplies the missing "insider" phenomenological map.

**Pages updated (~12–15)**:
- [[rudolph-gnosis-the-nature-and-history-of-gnosticism|Rudolph, *Gnosis*]] (new scholarship summary page in wiki/scholarship/ with full biblio, thesis, chapter summaries keyed to TOC, key contributions, explicit discussion of adds/challenges vs. prior wiki, cross-refs).
- [[gnostics|Gnostics]] (group; sources 2→3; last_updated; major new subsection "Rudolph's Phenomenological and Historical Synthesis" covering nature, history, sources, self-understanding, influence; cross-link to new scholarship page and new concepts/texts).
- [[valentinus|Valentinus]] (figure; sources 1→2; enhanced "Valentinian System" and added Rudolph treatment of the "great system," social/cultic dimensions, irenic potential in some texts like Ptolemy's *Letter to Flora*; cross-refs to new concepts).
- New concept pages: [[gnosis|Gnosis (saving knowledge)]] (core soteriology; definition, features, variations, relation to faith/works and other concepts); [[demiurge|Demiurge]] (inferior creator/OT God; myth, variations, challenges to orthodox creation theology); [[pleroma|Pleroma (divine fullness)]] (aeons, emanations, Sophia disruption, soteriological goal); [[sophia-gnostic|Sophia (Gnostic) / fall of Wisdom]] (pivotal myth, etiological and soteriological functions, biblical wisdom reinterpretation, school variations).
- New text page: [[nag-hammadi-library|Nag Hammadi Library]] (discovery, contents by school, revolutionary significance per Rudolph, major tractates, relation to heresiologists and canon formation; cross-refs throughout).
- [[index.md]] (expanded gnostics entry with Rudolph ref; added Rudolph to scholars list with full description; cross-refs to new pages).
- [[overview.md]] (new subsection on Rudolph/Gnosticism as scope expansion: phenomenological method, primary sources, historical breadth to Manichaeism/Mandaeans, challenges to "Christian heresy" framing, new pages created; updates to early Christianity/heterodox section and counts).
- Cross-links and light enhancements throughout (irenaeus, bauer-thesis, nt-canon-formation, rule-of-faith, woman-wisdom, principalities-powers, stoicheia, etc.).

**Total**: ~12–15 updates (1 new scholarship + 1 expanded group + 1 enhanced figure + 4 new concepts + 1 new text + 2 bookkeeping + cross-links). Wiki scope expansion: first dedicated, systematic treatment of Gnosticism as a late antique religious phenomenon with its own "nature" and history, centered on primary Nag Hammadi sources and phenomenological coherence rather than solely patristic reaction or sociological statistics. Adds depth to 2nd-c. diversity, introduces the Sophia/Demiurge/Pleroma/gnosis myth in dedicated pages, and positions Gnosticism as a major intersecting tradition alongside biblical, early Christian, and (recently) Kabbalistic materials.

**Bookkeeping**: index and overview refreshed; log appended. Hermeneutical tracking: Gnostic exegesis as esoteric/protest re-reading (inversion of Genesis creator, elevation of "knowledge" over faith/works, Sophia myth as etiological for evil and alienation); Rudolph's method (phenomenological description prior to history) noted and contrasted with evangelical grammatico-historical and patristic rule-of-faith approaches already in the wiki. New forward links created to existing and new pages; "Gnostic" now has balanced primary + secondary + synthetic coverage.

**Next frontier noted**: dedicated pages or expansions for specific Nag Hammadi tractates (e.g., *Apocryphon of John*, *Gospel of Truth*); Manichaeism as distinct tradition (Rudolph treats it as developed Gnosis); Mandaean texts/sources; further engagement with modern critiques of the "Gnosticism" category (Williams, King) or comparative work (Jonas' *The Gnostic Religion* as classic phenomenological predecessor to Rudolph). Also potential for controversies page on "definition of Gnosticism" or "Jewish vs. Christian origins of Gnosis." (Rudolph's bibliography and chronological table suggest further primary and secondary sources worth seeking.)

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Hecker, Joel (trans. and annot.). *The Zohar: Pritzker Edition, Volume Eleven: Midrash ha-Ne’lam and Zoharic Compositions*. Stanford University Press, 2019.

Source type: primary text / stratum of [[kabbalah|Kabbalah]], Joel Hecker’s annotated translation from the reconstructed critical Aramaic (Pritzker Edition). Volume XI is the first Pritzker volume to present a deliberate variety of Zoharic *genres* rather than the continuous running Torah commentary. It comprises additional *Midrash ha-Ne’lam* on the Megillot (Song of Songs, Ruth, Lamentations) plus the independent “Zoharic Compositions” (Matnitin, Toseftot, a *Zohar on Song of Songs*, and the *Idra Zuta* / “Small Assembly”). Hecker explicitly frames much of this material as belonging to the *earliest* phases of the Zoharic corpus, despite its later appearance in print under the title *Zohar Ḥadash* (“New Zohar”) — a label he calls misleading.

**Key contributions**:
(1) **Hecker’s framing of generic diversity and chronology**: “This eleventh volume in the series is the first to present a variety of Zoharic texts, reflecting the range of genres within the Zoharic library. Many of these sections first appeared in print in the publication that was titled *Zohar Ḥadash* … this title is misleading, however, in that the volume comprises some of the earliest Zoharic material.” The ingest foregrounds that the Zohar is a *library* or corpus with distinct voices and strata from the outset, not a single uniform commentary.
(2) **Additional Midrash ha-Ne’lam on the Megillot**: Extends the concealed, soul- and glory-centered midrash of Vol. X beyond Genesis. Sample from *Midrash ha-Ne’lam on Ruth*: “In the days when the judges judged … (Ruth 1:1). The rabbis explained the verse *When the morning stars sang together, and all the sons of God shouted for joy* (Job 38:7). Who are the morning stars? Holy angels ruling by day. Who are the sons of God? Angels ruling by night. … Everything that the blessed Holy One created, He created only for His glory, as is said: *Everyone who is called by My name, whom I created for My glory* (Isaiah 43:7).” These units deepen the treatment of exile, divine glory, angelic realms, and the soul in a “concealed” mode.
(3) **The Zoharic Compositions and the *Idra Zuta***: The independent theosophic units, above all the *Idra Zuta* (“Small Assembly”) — the intimate gathering in which Rabbi Shim’on bar Yoḥai (the Holy Lamp) discloses the most recondite secrets of the Godhead on the day of his death, his soul departing in the midst of the revelation. The assembly form itself is theurgic and revelatory; the circle of Companions enacts cosmic processes. This is widely regarded as the literary and theological pinnacle of the Zoharic corpus.
(4) **Hermeneutical and theurgic expansion**: Still operating at the Sod level of [[pardes|PaRDeS]], but now in the concentrated “assembly” and concealed-midrash genres. The theurgy of mystical fraternity and discourse is foregrounded (the very gathering and speech of the *Ḥavrayya* sustains and reveals the *zivvuga qaddisha* and the inner divine life). Visionary language around Shekhinah (e.g., “eye” signifying Shekhinah; colored lights of Gevurah too harsh to view directly; the mystic/prophet must close then open the eyes for partial vision) adds new phenomenological texture.
(5) **Cumulative portrait of the Zoharic corpus**: With Vols. X–XI the wiki now tracks the main running commentary (I–IX), the earliest concealed midrash (Vol. X + Megillot extensions), and the dramatic theosophic assemblies/compositions (Vol. XI). This fulfills the long-noted frontier of “remaining Zohar material (e.g., the Idra Zuta … other embedded homilies or later Pritzker volumes).”

**Contradictions / tensions flagged (not flattened)**: The “New Zohar” / *Zohar Ḥadash* print tradition presents some of the *earliest* material as “new” — a bibliographic irony that Hecker corrects and that the wiki now records. The concentrated, assembly-based theosophy of the *Idra Zuta* and Matnitin stands in productive tension with the more expansive, verse-anchored running commentary of the main strata (different literary form, even more esoteric register, yet shared sefirotic/theurgic/Sod commitments). No adjudication; both voices are preserved as part of the living Zoharic polyphony. The death of Rashbi in the *Idra Zuta* also sharpens the gap between the historical Tanna (halakhist) and the legendary mystagogue already noted on the figure page.

**Pages created (1 new)**: [[joel-hecker|Joel Hecker]] (commentator page — bio, role in Pritzker as translator of Vol. XI, detailed treatment of the additional *Midrash ha-Ne’lam* on the Megillot and the Zoharic Compositions/*Idra Zuta*, hermeneutical contribution, cross-links).

Hermeneutical tracking: explicit attention to the “assembly” and concealed-midrash modes as distinct yet complementary expressions of Sod within the same early corpus; the theurgic/performative dimension of the mystical circle itself is now foregrounded alongside the theurgy of mitsvot. New forward links created (especially to hecker and deepened Rashbi). All dates normalized to real system date 2026-06-07 per prior maintenance.

**Next frontier noted**: Vol. XII (Wolski, completing the Pritzker set); any remaining embedded Zoharic units or *Zohar Ḥadash* material not covered in XI; dedicated expansions for the *Idra Zuta* (perhaps its own controversy or concept page on the “mystical assembly” as genre); further integration of Megillot readings into [[ruth|Ruth]], [[lamentations]], and [[song-of-songs]] text pages; deeper work on the theurgic circle / Companions as a distinct “group” phenomenon; or sources on later Zoharic reception (Cordovero, Luria) that engage these specific units. The distinction between running commentary, concealed midrash, and assembly discourse is now explicitly tracked.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Wolski, Nathan and Hecker, Joel (trans. and annot.). *The Zohar: Pritzker Edition, Volume Twelve: Zoharic Compositions*. Stanford University Press, 2017.

Source type: primary text / stratum of [[kabbalah|Kabbalah]], the concluding block of the independent “Zoharic Compositions” in the Pritzker Edition critical translation (from reconstructed Aramaic; EPUB verified in `raw/commentaries/Zohar/The Zohar XII - Nathan Wolski.epub`). Volume XII completes the non-running-commentary esoteric strata begun in Vol. XI (Hecker). It carries a joint Introduction by Wolski and Hecker. Wolski translated and annotated Heikhalot Be-Reshit, Heikhalot Pequdei, and Piqqudin; Hecker the remainder (Raza de-Razin, Sitrei Otiyyot, Qav ha-Middah, Merkevet Yeḥezqel, Zohar al ha-Parashot, and passages mislabeled as Tosefta).

**Key contributions**:
(1) **Joint Introduction framing of the “library” and chronology**: “This volume presents a variety of Zoharic texts, reflecting the range of genres within the Zoharic library. Many of these sections appear in *Zohar Ḥadash*, or *New Zohar*; this title is a misnomer, however, since the volume comprises some of the earliest Zoharic material.” The ingest foregrounds (building on Vol. XI) that the Zohar is a stratified *corpus* with distinct voices from the outset rather than a single uniform work; the bibliographic label “New Zohar” for some of the most primordial strata is explicitly corrected.
(2) **Heikhalot theurgic prayer praxis (Wolski units)**: Two versions (shorter Be-Reshit; expanded Pequdei with halls of impurity). The halls are Malkhut/Shekhinah’s maidens or chambers; the worshiper “arranges and adorns them in preparation for their union with the sefirot, and ultimately for the union of the male and female grades of Divinity.” The “real story … is the story of Malkhut and Binah, the binding of ‘seventh with seventh.’” Prayer as active theurgic adornment that opens divine flow; patriarchs/Israel in distress arouse Shekhinah. New dedicated [[heikhalot|Heikhalot]] concept page created. Reimagination of classical Heikhalot literature through Zoharic sefirotic theosophy and theurgy.
(3) **Raza de-Razin (“Secret of Secrets”; Hecker unit)**: Physiognomy, metoposcopy, and chiromancy as Sod-level reading of the body as revelatory text (linked to *Secretum secretorum*). Clinical-style case notes on features and corresponding character/sins. Distinctive Zoharic contribution: **mutability** of physical features as direct result of virtuous/sinful behavior and *teshuvah* — “the Zohar’s perennial pietism … manifest in physical form.” New dedicated [[raza-de-razin|Raza de-Razin]] concept page created. Extends earlier Zoharic physiognomic passages.
(4) **Letter mysticism, emanation, and supplementary units (Hecker)**: *Sitrei Otiyyot* — the letters of YHVH “inscribed ethereally within the highest reaches of Divinity” engrave the sefirot on a heavenly tablet; the sefirot serve as “chariots” supporting and crowning the letters (core theogony and divine-name mysticism). *Qav ha-Middah* — the luminous “Line of Measure” from Ein Sof that gives scale, shape, light, color, and permanence to the sefirot (biblical measuring imagery cosmogonically transmuted). Additional: Piqqudin (commandments in theurgic/festival key, e.g. Ten Commandments on Shavu’ot, supernal shofar = Binah on Shabbat eve); Merkevet Yeḥezqel; Zohar al ha-Parashot; Tosefta-style addenda (Torah’s apotropaic protection against prosecuting angels, Moses’ staff as primordial item transformed at the bush and engraving Judgment, blending of Compassion and Judgment in Torah/Moses).
(5) **Completion of the Pritzker Edition and translator credits**: With this volume the full I–XII set (running commentary I–IX by Matt; concealed midrash X by Wolski + Megillot extensions in XI; Compositions XI–XII by Hecker and Wolski) is now represented. Clarified split responsibilities and joint Introduction on both commentator pages and the main zohar text page.
(6) **Hermeneutical expansion at the Sod level**: Prayer as adornment/union (Heikhalot), body as mutable readable text (Raza), letters as generative of divine structure (Sitrei Otiyyot), and measurement/emanation from the Infinite (Qav) as embodied and visual modalities of esoteric knowledge, complementing the dramatic assembly mode of the *Idra Zuta* (Vol. XI) and the systematic parashah theosophy/theurgy (Vols. I–IX).

**Contradictions / tensions flagged (not flattened)**: The “New Zohar” / *Zohar Ḥadash* print tradition presents some of the *earliest* material as “new” — the same bibliographic irony already noted for Vol. XI material, now extended and explicitly corrected from the joint Introduction. The concentrated, self-contained theosophic treatises stand in productive tension with the verse-anchored running commentary (different literary form and register, yet shared sefirotic/theurgic/Sod commitments and the central drama of Shekhinah union). No adjudication; both voices preserved as part of the Zoharic polyphony. The physiognomic “science” (with its mutability emphasis) also sits alongside broader Jewish ambivalence or prohibition of divination in other strata.

**Pages created (2 new)**: 
- [[heikhalot|Heikhalot (Zoharic)]] (concept page: definition, classical background vs. Zoharic theurgic reimagination, two versions, “seventh with seventh,” prayer as adornment, cross-links to zohar, shekhinah, sefirot, Wolski/Hecker, Rashbi).
- [[raza-de-razin|Raza de-Razin (Secret of Secrets)]] (concept page: physiognomy/metoposcopy/chiromancy as Sod body-reading, clinical notes, distinctive mutability/teshuvah/pietism emphasis, relation to letter mysticism and earlier Zoharic passages, cross-links).

**Pages updated (~6–8)**: 
- [[zohar|The Zohar]] (frontmatter tags/sources already at 12; lead list bullet for Vol. XII fully revised with translator split, full unit inventory, misnomer framing, and links to new concepts; summary ✅ paragraph updated; entire “## Structure of Volume XII” section replaced with accurate joint credits, key Introduction quote, expanded and corrected Major Units list including the two Heikhalot versions and mutability detail, and updated Relation paragraph).
- [[nathan-wolski|Nathan Wolski]] (Vol. XII section revised to specify exactly the three units he translated/annotated — Heikhalot Be-Reshit/Pequdei and Piqqudin — plus joint Introduction note and cross-refs to the Hecker-handled units and new concepts; Relation text lightly aligned).
- [[joel-hecker|Joel Hecker]] (frontmatter sources_ingested 1→2 + last_updated; new “## Contributions to Volume XII (Zoharic Compositions)” subsection detailing his units, the joint Introduction, and cross-refs; Sources Ingested list expanded with Vol. XII entry and summary paragraph updated; Relation section extended to note work across XI–XII).
- [[overview.md]] (Vol XII addendum paragraph revised for split credits, full units, misnomer point, new concept links, and cross-refs to both commentators).
- [[index.md]] (Zohar bullet revised with accurate translator split, complete units, misnomer note, and links to the two new concept pages).
- Cross updates/links throughout related pages (shekhinah, sefirot, ein-sof, theurgy themes, shimon-bar-yochai, zohar-authorship) via the new concepts and refined descriptions.
- [[log.md]] (this entry).

**Total**: 2 new + ~6–8 updated. Wiki scope: the independent Zoharic Compositions are now fully and accurately represented with translator credits, the “earliest strata” framing from the source itself, and dedicated concept pages for the two most distinctive new units (Heikhalot theurgic praxis and Raza de-Razin physiognomy). The Zohar is tracked as a multi-generic, stratified corpus with explicit attention to the non-running theosophic treatises.

**Bookkeeping**: index and overview refreshed with precise credits and new pages; both commentator pages now accurately reflect their contributions to Vols. XI–XII; log appended with full detail. All dates normalized per prior maintenance.

**Hermeneutical tracking**: Explicit attention to Sod in new embodied/visual modes — prayer as theurgic adornment of the divine feminine (Heikhalot), the body itself as a mutable text to be read and rewritten through ethical/repentant conduct (Raza), letters of the Name as generative of the sefirotic structure (Sitrei Otiyyot), and cosmogonic measurement from Ein Sof (Qav ha-Middah). These complement the “assembly” discourse of Vol. XI and the running parashah theosophy of I–IX while sharing the core commitment to the *zivvuga qaddisha* and human participation in the inner life of God. The joint Introduction’s reframing of the “New Zohar” print tradition as containing early material is recorded as a bibliographic/hermeneutical correction.

**Next frontier noted**: Later reception and commentary on these specific units (e.g., Cordovero’s *Pardes Rimmonim* or Lurianic elaborations on Heikhalot, physiognomy, or the Line of Measure); dedicated pages or expansions for the full “Zoharic Compositions” as a genre, the two Heikhalot versions in comparative detail, or the *Idra* literature as a whole; integration of relevant Megillot or parashah resonances from these units into the corresponding text/figure pages; possible controversy page on the status of physiognomy, chiromancy, and other “esoteric sciences” within Jewish tradition (biblical prohibitions vs. Zoharic and later kabbalistic valorization); or scholarly sources on the relationship between these Zoharic strata and classical Heikhalot or medieval physiognomic manuals. The distinction between running commentary, concealed midrash, assembly discourse, and these concentrated theosophic treatises is now explicitly tracked across the full Pritzker set.

## 2026-06-07 ingest | Wolski, Nathan (trans. and annot.). *The Zohar: Pritzker Edition, Volume Twelve: Zoharic Compositions*. Stanford University Press, 2019.

Source type: primary text / stratum of [[kabbalah|Kabbalah]], Nathan Wolski’s annotated translation from the reconstructed critical Aramaic (Pritzker Edition). Volume XII completes the presentation of the independent “Zoharic Compositions” (the non-running-commentary esoteric strata whose treatment began in Vol. XI with Hecker) and brings the entire Pritzker Edition (Vols. I–XII) into the wiki. Wolski, who previously handled the *Midrash ha-Ne’lam* in Vol. X, here supplies the final major block of concentrated theosophic treatises: *Heikhalot* (halls), *Raza de-Razin* (physiognomy), *Sitrei Otiyyot* (secrets of the letters), *Qav ha-Middah* (line of measure), Tosefta addenda, and festival-oriented units.

**Key contributions**:
(1) **Completion of the Pritzker Edition and the “Zoharic Compositions”**: With this volume the wiki now holds the full critical English Pritzker set. The Compositions (Vols. XI–XII) stand alongside the main running Torah commentary (I–IX) and the concealed midrashic layers (X–XI) as a distinct generic voice — self-contained theosophic events rather than parashah exegesis.
(2) **Heikhalot and the theurgy of adornment/union**: Expansive treatment of the divine halls as Malkhut/Shekhinah’s maidens or chambers, adorned through prayer and ritual for union with the upper sefirot and Binah (“seventh with seventh”). “The worshiper arranges and adorns them in preparation for their union with the sefirot, and ultimately for the union of the male and female grades of Divinity.” The seventh hall is hidden and concealed; a multi-colored pillar stands within it. Patriarchs and Israel in distress arouse Shekhinah for protection. Deepens the central *zivvuga qaddisha* drama and the theurgic role of prayer/ritual.
(3) **Letter mysticism and emanation (Sitrei Otiyyot, Qav ha-Middah)**: The letters of YHVH inscribed ethereally in highest Divinity engrave the sefirot on a heavenly tablet; the sefirot function as “chariots” supporting and crowning the letters. The *Qav ha-Middah* (luminous “Line of Measure” from Ein Sof) gives scale, shape, light, color, and permanence to the sefirot. Core texts for theogony and the process by which Ein Sof structures the divine realm.
(4) **Esoteric knowledge and protection (Raza de-Razin, Tosefta, festival units)**: Physiognomy, metoposcopy, and chiromancy (*Raza de-Razin*, linked to *Secretum secretorum*) treat the body as a revelatory text revealing character, temperament, and fate. Tosefta and related units address Torah’s apotropaic power against prosecuting angels/enemies, the staff of Moses (primordial item, transformed at the bush, engraving Judgment), the blending of Compassion and Judgment, the Ten Commandments in Shavu’ot context, and the supernal shofar (Binah) on Shabbat eve.
(5) **Cumulative portrait of the Zoharic corpus and hermeneutics**: With Vols. X–XII the wiki tracks the full range: running commentary (I–IX, systematic theosophy/theurgy on the entire Torah), concealed midrash (X–XI, soul/glory/exile on Genesis + Megillot), and independent compositions (XI–XII, Heikhalot halls, letter secrets, physiognomy, measure, addenda). All operate at the Sod level of [[pardes|PaRDeS]] but in distinct literary and performative modes. The theurgy of the mystical circle (assemblies) and of prayer/ritual (adornment of Shekhinah, protection) is now richly documented alongside the theurgy of mitsvot in the main commentary.

**Contradictions / tensions flagged (not flattened)**: The “New Zohar” / *Zohar Ḥadash* bibliographic tradition presents some of the earliest and most primordial material as “new” — an irony already noted in Vol. XI and here reinforced by the completion of the Compositions. The concentrated, treatise-like form of the Compositions (letter mysticism, physiognomy, halls) stands in productive tension with both the expansive running commentary and the dramatic assembly dialogue of the *Idra Zuta*, yet shares the same sefirotic, theurgic, and Sod commitments. No adjudication; the wiki preserves the polyphony of the corpus.

**Pages created/updated (~6–8)**: 
- [[zohar|The Zohar]] (frontmatter sources 11→12 + tags; lead list extended with Vol. XII; new full “## Structure of Volume XII (Nathan Wolski)” section with framing, breakdown of Heikhalot/Raza de-Razin/Sitrei Otiyyot/Qav ha-Middah/Tosefta/etc. with verbatim samples, themes, relation to I–XI, and updated summary ✅ note; Cross-Links and Key Commentators enhanced with Wolski/Hecker for the compositions).
- [[nathan-wolski|Nathan Wolski]] (sources 1→2; lead and bio revised to cover both Vol. X and Vol. XII; new dedicated section on the Zoharic Compositions with units, samples, and characteristics; Relation, Cross-Links, and Sources Ingested updated; tags +zoharic-compositions).
- [[overview.md]] (top Jewish Mysticism scope paragraph refreshed for full Pritzker I–XII/complete; long history paragraph updated and **Vol XII addendum** added after the XI one with framing, key units, completion note, and cross-links).
- [[index.md]] (Zohar bullet revised to include Vol. XII and completion of Pritzker; Wolski commentator entry updated for both volumes).
- [[log.md]] (this entry).
- Cross updates via new/strengthened links to shekhinah, sefirot, ein-sof, theurgy, divine-name, zohar-authorship, etc.

**Total**: 0 new pages + ~6–8 updated/enhanced. Wiki scope expansion: completion of the full Pritzker Edition (I–XII). The Zohar is now tracked as a complete multi-generic corpus (running commentary + concealed midrash + independent theosophic compositions) with rich documentation of its theurgy, letter mysticism, divine feminine, and esoteric knowledge traditions. The long-noted frontier for the Pritzker set is fulfilled.

**Bookkeeping**: index, overview, and zohar refreshed; nathan-wolski page substantially enhanced; log appended. Hermeneutical tracking: explicit attention to the composition mode (halls as theurgic adornment, letters as generators of sefirot, physiognomy as sod, measure from Ein Sof) alongside prior modes. All dates normalized to real system date 2026-06-07. Raw sources untouched.

**Next frontier noted**: later Zoharic reception and commentary (e.g., Moses Cordovero *Or Yaqar*, Isaac Luria and the transformation of these units via tsimtsum/shevirah/tikkun); dedicated expansions for specific compositions (e.g., a concept page for “Raza de-Razin”/physiognomy or “Heikhalot” if sustained new treatment emerges); integration of Vol. XII material into existing concepts (shekhinah, sefirot, ein-sof, theurgy, divine-name) or text pages (e.g., festival/Shavu’ot dimensions); or sources on the historical transmission and printing of these strata (*Zohar Ḥadash*). The distinction between running commentary, concealed midrash, and independent composition is now fully tracked across the Pritzker set.

## [2026-06-09] migration | Schema restructure — sects as first-class pages, canon_scope, two-level traditions/

**Type**: structural migration (not a source ingest). No `raw/` files touched; no prior log
entries altered. Directed by user to solve three compounding organizational problems:
(1) canonical status was frontmatter, not a navigable structural dimension; (2) sects had no
first-class page type; (3) flat `traditions/` would not scale across world religions.

**CLAUDE.md revised** (approved before execution):
- `traditions/` documented as a **two-level hierarchy**: `traditions/<tradition>/<tradition>.md`
  + `traditions/<tradition>/sects/<sect>.md`.
- Text Page schema: added structured **`canon_scope`** block (`canonical_for` /
  `deuterocanonical_for` / `authoritative_for` / `disputed_by` / `rejected_by`, entries =
  tradition/sect slugs); `canon_status` demoted to a coarse headline; required **Canon and
  Reception** body section, with Gospel of Thomas and Book of Mormon as worked stress cases.
- Added two new page-type schemas: **Tradition Page** (none existed before) and
  **Sect / Denomination Page** (parent_tradition, canon-divergence, doctrinal distinctives,
  relationship_to_orthodoxy).
- Updated Hermeneutical Tracking, Ingest Workflow (step 1 now requires the canon-status-within-
  sect-vs-parent question), Lint Workflow, and Naming/Linking (group vs sect vs tradition
  disambiguation; nested paths for filing only; canon_scope entries are slugs).

**Pages migrated/created**:
- Restructured `traditions/`: created [[judaism|Judaism]] and [[christianity|Christianity]]
  tradition overview pages; `git mv` [[rabbinic-judaism]] and [[kabbalah]] into
  `traditions/judaism/sects/` and reframed their frontmatter to the sect schema.
- New sect stubs (8): Christianity — [[proto-orthodox-christianity]], [[gnosticism]],
  [[valentinianism]], [[sethianism]], [[marcionism]], [[montanism]], [[ebionism]];
  Judaism — [[karaism]].
- Cross-linked the four overlapping `groups/` pages ([[gnostics]], [[marcionites]],
  [[montanists]], [[ebionites]]) to their new sect pages (kept both per group/sect convention).
- `canon_scope` rolled out to the **12 contested/sectarian text pages**: [[nag-hammadi-library]]
  (canonical for Gnostic sects, rejected by proto-orthodoxy); the seven Apostolic Fathers
  ([[1-clement]], [[2-clement]], [[didache]], [[epistle-of-barnabas]], [[shepherd-of-hermas]],
  [[epistle-to-diognetus]], [[fragments-of-papias]]); and the antilegomena / disputed
  canonical books [[esther]], [[jude]], [[2-peter]], [[revelation]]. Firmly-canonical Tanakh/NT
  books deferred for per-ingest backfill (full Canon-and-Reception body sections likewise
  deferred for the Apostolic Fathers).

**Bookkeeping**: `index.md` Traditions section rebuilt as "Traditions and Sects" (two-level,
nested); `overview.md` Coverage table updated (Traditions = 2 overviews; new Sects row = 10)
with a dated structural note; total ~284 → ~295+. All dates normalized to 2026-06-09.

**Follow-ups noted**: backfill `canon_scope` on the remaining ~63 text pages organically per
ingest; add full Canon-and-Reception body sections to the Apostolic Fathers; create sect pages
for later denominations (Catholicism, Eastern Orthodoxy, Protestantism, Latter-day Saints) and
tradition pages for non-Abrahamic religions as sources arrive; the forward-referenced slugs
`catholicism`, `eastern-orthodoxy`, `protestantism`, `thomasine-christianity`, `coptic-christianity`,
and `latter-day-saints` currently resolve to not-yet-created pages by design.

list. Sources (public domain, in `raw/`): Dhammapada (Buddharakkhita), Heart Sutra (Conze), Lotus Sutra
(Kern), Visuddhimagga (Nanamoli, PDF), Bodhicaryavatara (Barnett).

- Tradition: [[buddhism|Buddhism]]. Sects: [[theravada|Theravada]], [[mahayana|Mahayana]],
  [[vajrayana|Vajrayana]] — the **Theravada/Mahayana canon split** documented (Pali Canon alone vs. the
  Mahayana sutras).
- Texts: [[tipitaka|Pali Canon]], [[dhammapada|Dhammapada]] (`canonical_for [theravada]`,
  `authoritative_for [mahayana, vajrayana]`); [[heart-sutra|Heart Sutra]], [[lotus-sutra|Lotus Sutra]]
  (`canonical_for [mahayana, vajrayana]`, `rejected_by [theravada]`) — the cleanest multi-canon use of
  `canon_scope` so far.
- Concepts: [[four-noble-truths|Four Noble Truths]], [[anatta|Anatta]], [[sunyata|Sunyata]],
  [[bodhisattva|Bodhisattva]], [[neyartha-nitartha|Neyartha/Nitartha]].
- Figure: [[gautama-buddha|The Buddha]]. Commentators: [[buddhaghosa|Buddhaghosa]] (Visuddhimagga),
  [[shantideva|Shantideva]] (Bodhicaryavatara).

**Cross-tradition fault lines flagged**: [[anatta|non-self]] vs. the Hindu *atman* ([[upanishads]]) and
the Abrahamic/Kabbalist soul; [[sunyata|emptiness]] vs. Brahman/[[ein-sof|Ein Sof]] and apophatic
theology. Several comparison pages flagged.

**Bookkeeping**: index.md (Traditions-and-Sects + Texts + Concepts + Figures + Commentators), overview.md

exemplar. `raw/` untouched.

Buddhism's multi-canon split. Sources (public domain, in `raw/`): Upanishads (Müller SBE1), Bhagavad
Gita (Arnold), Brahma Sutras with **Shankara** (Thibaut SBE34) and **Ramanuja** (Thibaut SBE48)
commentaries, Yoga Sutras (Johnston).

- Sects (the Vedanta schools): [[advaita-vedanta|Advaita]], [[vishishtadvaita-vedanta|Vishishtadvaita]],
  [[dvaita-vedanta|Dvaita]] — divergence is **interpretive, not canonical** (`canon_scope` identical).
- Texts: [[upanishads|Upanishads]], [[bhagavad-gita|Gita]], [[brahma-sutras|Brahma Sutras]],
  [[yoga-sutras|Yoga Sutras]] (`canonical_for: [hinduism]`).
- Commentators: [[shankara|Shankara]], [[ramanuja|Ramanuja]], [[madhva|Madhva]] (last a stub — no free
  translation of his Brahma-Sutra commentary).
- Concepts: [[brahman|Brahman]], [[atman|Atman]], [[moksha|Moksha]], [[maya|Maya]]. Figure: [[krishna|Krishna]].
- Controversy (flagged triad): [[brahman-atman-vedanta-schools|The Nature of Brahman and the Self]] —
  Advaita/Vishishtadvaita/Dvaita on "tat tvam asi."

**Cross-tradition wiring**: [[atman|atman]] ↔ Buddhist [[anatta|non-self]] and [[brahman|Brahman]] ↔
[[sunyata|emptiness]]/[[ein-sof|Ein Sof]] now connect the Indian and other traditions; the
[[upanishads|Upanishads]] forward-refs from the Buddhism pages are resolved.

**Bookkeeping**: index.md (Traditions-and-Sects + Texts + Concepts + Figures + Commentators +
Controversies), overview.md (Traditions 5→6, Sects 22→25, total ~349+). `raw/` untouched.

## [2026-06-09] ingest | Daoism — Daodejing + Zhuangzi (Legge); the Wang Bi/Heshang Gong divide

Tier 2 cont. The most hermeneutically foreign tradition so far. CLAUDE.md updated first with the
**commentary-as-transformation** framework (Hermeneutical Tracking). Sources (public domain, in `raw/`):
Daodejing (Legge SBE39) and Zhuangzi (Legge SBE39–40). The classical commentaries (Wang Bi, Heshang Gong,
Guo Xiang) are all untranslated/copyright — documented from secondary knowledge.

**Pages created (12):**
- Tradition: [[daoism|Daoism]]. Sects: [[philosophical-daoism|Philosophical]] (*daojia*) and
  [[religious-daoism|Religious]] (*daojiao*).
- Texts: [[daodejing|Daodejing]], [[zhuangzi|Zhuangzi]] (`canonical_for: [daoism]`).
- Concepts: [[dao|Dao]], [[wu-wei|Wu-wei]], [[ziran|Ziran]]. Figure: [[laozi|Laozi]].
- Commentators: [[wang-bi|Wang Bi]] (philosophical/xuanxue) and [[heshang-gong|Heshang Gong]]
  (longevity-cultivation) — both stubs pending translations.
- Controversy (flagged): [[wang-bi-vs-heshang-gong|Wang Bi vs. Heshang Gong]].

**Methodological note**: Daoism gives the wiki its **third distinct pattern of scriptural division** —
same canon, different *purpose of reading* (metaphysics vs. body-cultivation) — alongside Buddhism's
multi-canon split and Hindu Vedanta's one-canon-opposite-metaphysics. The ineffable [[dao|Dao]] is now
cross-linked to the other "beyond-name" ultimates ([[ein-sof|Ein Sof]], [[sunyata|emptiness]],
[[brahman|nirguna Brahman]]).

**Bookkeeping**: index.md (all sections), overview.md (Traditions 6→7, Sects 25→27, total ~361+).
`raw/` untouched. (Daoist concept pages forward-reference [[analects|Confucian li]] — resolved with the
Confucianism ingest, next.)

  trademark-holding) and [[free-zone-scientology|Free Zone]] (independents / "squirrels") — a real
  parent-vs-schism dispute over *who keeps the tech*.

spark.

  birth/Titanomachy/Typhon/loves; [[hera]] persecutions + Judgment of Paris; [[poseidon]] Athens-contest +
  Odyssey wrath; [[demeter]] the rape of Persephone; [[athena]] birth/Arachne; [[apollo]] Delos/Python/Daphne;

New pages: commentator `john-hick`; concepts `religious-pluralism` (Race's exclusivism/inclusivism/pluralism typology; pluralism vs. perennialism; the soteriological criterion; mythological vs. literal truth), `the-real` (neo-Kantian Real *an sich* vs. as humanly experienced; personae/impersonae; nirguna/saguna Brahman, dharmata/upaya Dharmakaya parallels; the ineffability objection), `axial-age` (Jaspers's Achsenzeit; pre-axial cosmic vs. post-axial soteriological religion; cosmic optimism), `religious-realism` (critical realism vs. non-realism — Feuerbach, Braithwaite, Randall, Phillips, Cupitt); controversy `religious-pluralism-debate` (the five positions; the fidelity-vs-parity crux; seeded for Armstrong + Prothero extension). Cross-links: the Real reframes tawhid/Trinity/brahman/dao/ein-sof/sunyata as phenomenal manifestations; pluralism debate ties to crucifixion-in-the-quran, brahman-atman-vedanta-schools, anatta/atman, tawhid; theodicy (Hick's soul-making). Bookkeeping: index.md (new Commentators + Concepts + Controversies subsections), overview.md (new meta-level scope paragraph; noted calibre "Quick Start Guide" in raw/misc is software docs, NOT ingested). No separate scholarship/ summary page (per "commentator pages" choice) — primary-source references kept as plain text to avoid dangling links. **Next in queue: Karen Armstrong, *A History of God* (developmental/experiential); then Stephen Prothero, *God Is Not One* (particularist counter-case).**

## [2026-06-09] ingest | Karen Armstrong, *A History of God* (philosophy of religion — tier 2 of 3 comparative-religion sources in raw/misc)
Second meta-level comparative source. Ingested Armstrong's *A History of God: The 4,000-Year Quest of Judaism, Christianity and Islam* (1993), filed (per the standing "commentator pages" direction) as commentator `karen-armstrong`. Her thesis: a history of the human *idea/experience* of God, not of God itself — no linear evolution, recurring themes; religion is **pragmatic** (an idea of God survives because it *works*, is discarded when it stops); the governing contrast between the **personal God** (anthropomorphic, idol-prone) and the **God of the mystics** (apophatic, ineffable, deliberately cultivated within), which she privileges; fundamentalism as a modern, antihistorical **mythos-as-logos** error; *Homo religiosus*. New concept pages: `mythos-and-logos` (Armstrong's two modes of knowing; myth as non-literal symbolic truth; overlaps Hick's literal/mythological truth, PaRDeS, zahir/batin, neyartha/nitartha) and `apophatic-theology` (the *via negativa*; personal God vs. God of the mystics; the suspect "God of the philosophers"; Ein Sof, nirguna Brahman, batin, essence/energies). Extended controversy `religious-pluralism-debate` with **§6 Developmental-Experiential (Armstrong)** and updated its scope note. **Contradiction Protocol**: Armstrong privileges the same apophatic Real as Hick but drifts toward the **non-realism** (Cupitt-adjacent) that Hick wrote to refute — flagged on `karen-armstrong`, `religious-realism` (added Armstrong as near-non-realist; sources 1→2), and the debate page. Also cross-linked/updated `axial-age` (Armstrong adopts + expands the thesis in *The Great Transformation*; sources 1→2). Bookkeeping: index.md (added Armstrong to Scholarship commentators; added mythos-and-logos + apophatic-theology to Philosophy-of-Religion concepts), overview.md (extended the meta-level paragraph), this log. No separate scholarship/ summary page; primary-source reference kept as plain text. **Next in queue: Stephen Prothero, *God Is Not One* (the particularist counter-case to both Hick and Armstrong).**

## [2026-06-09] ingest | Stephen Prothero, *God Is Not One* (philosophy of religion — tier 3 of 3 comparative-religion sources in raw/misc; queue complete)
Third and final meta-level comparative source, the **particularist** counter-case to both Hick and Armstrong. Ingested Prothero's *God Is Not One: The Eight Rival Religions That Run the World — and Why Their Differences Matter* (2010), filed (per the standing "commentator pages" direction) as commentator `stephen-prothero`. Thesis: "**God is not one**" — the traditions converge somewhat on *ethics* but diverge sharply on doctrine, ritual, mythology, experience, and law, and those differences *matter*. The unitary view (he calls it "**Godthink**") comes in two forms — [[perennialism]] ("pretend pluralism": Huston Smith, Huxley, Campbell, and, he names her, Armstrong) and the New Atheists — both of which he charges with doing **theology disguised as neutral description**. New concept pages: `perennialism` (the *philosophia perennis*; many-paths-up-one-mountain; the Küng "anonymous Christians" critique turned on the perennialists; promoted from inline material on the debate page) and `four-part-model-of-religion` (his **problem → solution → technique → exemplar** scheme; "only Christians seek salvation"; the baseball/"scoring runs" analogy). Extended controversy `religious-pluralism-debate`: rewrote §5 Particularism (Prothero now ingested, no longer "pending"), updated the scope note (the three sources now a "pluralism trilogy"), §4 Perennialism (linked the new page; added Prothero filing Armstrong there), Cross-Links, and frontmatter `positions`. Also lightly wired `religious-pluralism` (added perennialism/particularism/Prothero links; sources 1→2). **Contradiction Protocol**: Prothero vs. Hick (rejects the shared-soteriology/"Real" frame as imposed) and Prothero vs. Armstrong (her mystical-core convergence is "assumed rather than found") — recorded on `stephen-prothero` and the debate page (§5 vs §3/§6), not adjudicated.

New tradition coverage: Prothero's ch. 6 elevates **Yoruba religion** to a "great religion," so built the wiki's first **West African** tradition — `yoruba-religion` (the way of the orishas; problem=disconnection/goal=connection; *ashe* = "power-to-make-things-happen"; the morally double-edged orishas; Olodumare, Eshu, Orunmila, Oshun, Obatala, Ogun, Shango et al.; *ori* and destiny; Ile-Ife) — with 2 diaspora sects (`santeria` [Cuban Regla de Ocha/Lucumí; orisha–saint syncretism; the Hialeah sacrifice case] and `candomble` [Brazilian; the *terreiro*; "danced religion"; matriarchal *mães-de-santo*]) and 1 text (`ifa-corpus`, the oral 256-*odu* divinatory canon; canonical for all three Yoruba pages). Bookkeeping: index.md (added Prothero to Scholarship commentators; perennialism + four-part-model to Philosophy-of-Religion concepts; new Yoruba Religion tradition block + sects; Ifa corpus under Texts), overview.md (extended meta-level paragraph — trilogy complete; Yoruba added), this log. No separate scholarship/ summary page; primary-source reference kept as plain text. Dangling-link check: see verification below. **Queue complete — all three raw/misc comparative-religion sources (Hick, Armstrong, Prothero) ingested.** Flagged for future: the individual orishas (Eshu, Ogun, Shango, Oshun, Obatala, Orunmila, Olodumare …) are candidates for `figures/` pages on the wiki's deity-figure convention, not yet written.

## [2026-06-09] ingest | Shai Secunda, *The Iranian Talmud* (Talmudo-Iranica — Bavli in its Sasanian context)
Ingested Shai Secunda's *The Iranian Talmud: Reading the Bavli in Its Sasanian Context* (Penn Press, 2014), an academic monograph from `raw/misc`. Unlike the Hick/Armstrong/Prothero trade books (commentator-only), this fits the wiki's established **scholarship + commentator** pattern (cf. Hurtado, Cohn, Rudolph, Wolfson, Skjærvø), so filed both. Thesis: the **[[talmud-bavli|Babylonian Talmud]]** is a product of **Sasanian Iran** and must be read against its Zoroastrian/Middle Persian neighbors — the comparative subfield **Talmudo-Iranica** (after Yaakov Elman). ## [2026-06-09] ingest | Andrew Delbanco, *The Death of Satan* (modern reception/secularization of evil)
Ingested Andrew Delbanco's *The Death of Satan: How Americans Have Lost the Sense of Evil* (Farrar, Straus and Giroux, 1995) from `raw/misc` (scanned PDF, 296 pp.; OCR via pdftotext). A **trade book** of literary/cultural history, so filed on the Armstrong/Prothero/Hick pattern (commentator page carrying the book summary; **no** separate scholarship/ summary page) — not the Secunda/Cohn academic-monograph scholarship+commentator pattern. Thesis: a "national spiritual biography" of how secularizing America **"unnamed" evil** — the personified Devil decayed from the vivid Puritan "Age of Belief" through the Enlightenment, the Romantic "birth of the self," the modern "Age of Blame," and the "Culture of Irony," leaving moderns confronting atrocity (Arendt's banality of evil) with "a sense of evil but no longer the language to talk intelligently about evil" (Sontag). Told through American literature (Melville the "polar star"; Edwards, Emerson, Hawthorne, Lincoln). New pages: figure **`satan`** (the wiki's first Satan/Devil page — a long-overdue cross-tradition figure; *ha-satan* the office/Accuser in Job/Zech/1 Chr → Second Temple Mastema/Belial/1 Enoch → NT/patristic fallen-angel Devil + the "Lucifer" construction from Isa 14 → Augustine's privation/free-will account + Milton → Islamic Iblis/Shaytan as jinn → Kabbalistic Samael; subordinated to God vs. Zoroastrian Ahriman; closes with Delbanco's "death of Satan"); commentator **`andrew-delbanco`**; concept **`secularization-of-evil`** (title "The Death of Satan / Secularization of Evil" — banality of evil, age of blame, belief vs. irony, "we cannot live without metaphor"; the modern phase succeeding classical theodicy). Updated: `theodicy` (new "Modern Phase: From 'Is God Just?' to 'Is There Evil?'" section + cross-links; last_updated bumped, sources unchanged), `antichrist` (Satan figure cross-link), `sitra-ahra` (Satan cross-link — Samael as the Jewish "satan"). Bookkeeping: index.md (Satan to Cross-Tradition figures; Delbanco to Philosophy/Comparative commentators; secularization-of-evil to Biblical-Theology concepts), overview.md, this log. **Resolves the pre-existing dangling `[[satan|Devil]]` link** flagged in prior lints (it sat in the Cohn paragraph of `zoroastrian-influence-on-abrahamic-eschatology`) — now points at the new figure page. ## [2026-06-09] ingest | Jon D. Levenson, *Resurrection and the Restoration of Israel* (resurrection's biblical roots; the Canaanite counter-thesis to Persian influence)
Ingested Jon D. Levenson's *Resurrection and the Restoration of Israel: The Ultimate Victory of the God of Life* (Yale UP, 2006) from `raw/misc` — the last of the three `raw/misc` works (queue now empty). A major academic monograph by a Harvard Jewish biblical theologian, so filed on the **scholarship + commentator** pattern (cf. Secunda, Cohn, Hurtado). Thesis: the **resurrection of the dead** is neither a Christian innovation, nor marginal/dispensable to Judaism, nor (mainly) a late foreign Zoroastrian import, but a "weight-bearing beam" of rabbinic Judaism that grew **slowly and organically** from native biblical roots — God's power over death (1 Sam 2:6), the non-universality of Sheol (fortunate vs. unfortunate death), familial continuity (infertility=death, restored progeny=resurrection), the Elijah/Elisha resurrection-miracles, national restoration (Ezekiel's dry bones), and, deepest, the **Canaanite Baal–Mot / Divine Warrior** pattern. Daniel 12:1–3 (+ Isa 26:19, the Servant of Isa 52–53) is the first explicit canonical affirmation; the Maccabean martyr crisis and Zoroastrian milieu were "catalysts, not reagents." New pages: scholarship `levenson-resurrection-and-the-restoration-of-israel` (chapter-by-chapter; the consensus he rejects; the resurrection/immortality-of-the-soul table; the Zoroastrian qualification); commentator `jon-d-levenson` (Jewish biblical theology = historical criticism + theology + rabbinic exegesis; against anachronistic individualism; major works incl. *Sinai and Zion*, *Creation and the Persistence of Evil*); concept `resurrection-of-the-dead` (bodily/communal/eschatological; Mishnah Sanhedrin 10:1; the Amidah *Gevurot*; Sadducee/Pharisee dispute; modern Reform shift to immortality-of-the-soul); concept `sheol` (the netherworld; Levenson's fortunate/unfortunate-death and non-universality argument). **Contradiction Protocol**: Levenson vs. the influence thesis on resurrection *specifically* — recorded on `zoroastrian-influence-on-abrahamic-eschatology` (new "Levenson — Resurrection's Roots Are Canaanite, Not Persian" section: Zoroastrianism "at best the grandmother"/"collateral relation," John Day's burial-and-waking argument), explicitly noting he and **[[cohn-cosmos-chaos-and-the-world-to-come|Cohn]] pull in opposite directions** (Cohn credits Zoroaster with the linear-eschatological frame; Levenson roots resurrection in the Canaanite Divine Warrior) — not adjudicated; partly a difference of scope (apocalyptic frame vs. resurrection doctrine). Other updates: `the-combat-myth` (Divine Warrior as resurrection's root), `mesopotamian-underworld` (cross-link to Sheol), `resurrection-body` controversy (the Jewish background; illuminates Option C — resurrection bound to covenant peoplehood), `elisha` (the Shunammite's son as the most extensive narrative resurrection). Bookkeeping: index.md (Levenson book to Hebrew-Bible-related/Jewish scholarship + Jewish-Tradition commentators; resurrection-of-the-dead + sheol to Biblical-Theology concepts), overview.md, this log. Dangling-link check: see verification below. **All three `raw/misc` works (Iranian Talmud, Delbanco, Levenson) now ingested — queue complete.** Flagged for future (not written): a `groups/` or stand-alone treatment is unnecessary (the `sadducees` sect page already exists and is cross-linked); a possible `concepts/divine-warrior` page (currently folded into `the-combat-myth`); `figures/baal` / Canaanite religion remain uncovered.

## [2026-06-09] revisit | Dasgupta Ch. XXVI (Madhva's Brahma-Sūtra interpretation) — deepened on close reading
Re-read **Chapter XXVI** of Vol. IV in full (read the prior ingest had summarized only its opening lineage paragraph). The chapter is a sūtra-by-sūtra (*adhikaraṇa*-by-*adhikaraṇa*) comparison of Madhva's vs. Śaṅkara's Brahma-Sūtra readings, worked through Jaya-tīrtha's *Nyāya-sudhā* and Vyāsa-tīrtha's *Tātparya-candrikā*.
- Added a substantial new section to `scholarship/dasgupta-history-of-indian-philosophy.md` — **"Inside Chapter XXVI: Madhva's Brahma-Sūtra Interpretation, Adhikaraṇa by Adhikaraṇa"** — covering BS I.1.1 (*athāto brahma-jijñāsā*, the theistic re-keying of every particle), the reality-of-bondage / anti-*ajñāna* polemic (the Dvaita counter to [[adhyasa|adhyāsa]]), the refutation of the *anirvacanīya* / *sad-asad-vilakṣaṇa* status of the world, BS I.1.2 (*janmādy asya yataḥ*, the **eight** divine functions; the *svarūpa*- vs *taṭastha-lakṣaṇa* dispute; the **_viśeṣa_** doctrine as Madhva's replacement for *samavāya*; *mukhyārtha*-by-difference on *tat tvam asi*), BS I.1.4 (*samanvaya*: every Upaniṣadic word denotes Viṣṇu; *śāstra* excludes the Śaiva Āgamas), the general pattern across I.1.5–I.4 (*vidvad-rūḍhi*; "Viṣṇu specifically"), the *māyāvāda*-worse-than-Buddhism polemic, and Dasgupta's philological note that Madhva re-divides and even adds sūtras.
- **Faithfulness correction:** the earlier pages glossed Madhva's *tat tvam asi* reading as the popular re-parsing *atat tvam asi* ("thou art *not* that"). Dasgupta's Ch. XXVI does **not** present that; he gives the *mukhyārtha*-by-difference vs. *lakṣaṇā* argument. Corrected `commentators/madhva.md` (hermeneutics), `traditions/hinduism/sects/dvaita-vedanta.md` (hermeneutics), and the *tat tvam asi* row of `comparisons/brahman-atman-vedanta-schools.md` to lead with Dasgupta's actual account and flag the re-parsing as a separate later-Mādhva move not in Dasgupta.
- Extended `texts/brahma-sutras.md` with a Madhva-reads-the-first-sutras paragraph (genuinely grounded in Ch. XXVI).

## 2026-06-09 ingest | Vermes, Geza. *The Complete Dead Sea Scrolls in English*. Penguin Classics, 7th rev. ed. 2012.

Source type: primary text + scholarship — the standard English translation and introduction of the Qumran corpus, by the foremost DSS scholar of the 20th century. Covers all major sectarian texts, biblical commentaries (pesharim), rewritten biblical texts, liturgical/wisdom texts, Copper Scroll, and an extensive catalogue appendix. Read in full: lines 1–22830 (across two sessions; final pass lines 17034–22830 in this session).

**Coverage**: Read in full. Lines 18519+ (Scroll Catalogue, Bibliography, Endnotes) are reference appendices read for completeness but not treated as primary analytical content.

**Key contributions**:

(1) **Essene hypothesis and Qumran community identity** — Vermes confidently identifies the Qumran community with the Essenes (Josephus, Philo, Pliny); solar calendar dispute, Zadokite priestly identity, Teacher of Righteousness, Wicked Priest (= Jonathan Maccabaeus, 153–143 BCE). (2) **Two Spirits doctrine** — complete predestinarian dualism (1QS III-IV); Sons of Light vs. Darkness; Prince of Light vs. Belial; Zoroastrian parallel; NT parallels (John, Paul, 1 Thess). (3) **Two Messiahs** — Messiah of Aaron (priestly, precedence) and Messiah of Israel (Davidic/royal); together with a Prophet (Deut 18:18); detailed in messianic banquet (1QSa). (4) **Pesher hermeneutic** — all prophetic texts are contemporizing fulfillments of community's own history; God revealed the full meaning of prophecy to the Teacher; five types of Qumran commentary. (5) **Sanctuary of Men** — community = human temple; works of the Law substitute for Temple sacrifice (4Q174 *miqdash 'adam*). (6) **Heavenly Melchizedek (11Q13)** — eschatological *elohim*/archangel; releases captives at tenth Jubilee; judges against Belial/Melkiresha'. (7) **Messianic Apocalypse (4Q521)** — explicit Qumran resurrection; "He will revive the dead and bring good news to the poor" (cf. Matt 11:2-6). (8) **Angelic Liturgy (4Q400-407)** — earliest post-biblical Merkabah mysticism; seven heavenly sanctuaries; seven Prince-Priests. (9) **MMT** — "works of the Law" as specific halakhic acts; the Pauline parallel. (10) **Jubilees at Qumran** — Hebrew original confirmed; 4Q225 = earliest pre-Christian Isaac voluntary self-sacrifice. (11) **Book of Giants** — Gilgamesh named; no Book of Parables at Qumran.

## 2026-06-10 ingest | Thibaut, George (trans.). *The Vedānta-Sūtras with the Commentary by Rāmānuja* (Śrī-Bhāṣya). Sacred Books of the East Vol. 48. Oxford, 1904.

Source type: primary commentary (translation). **Coverage: read in full — lines 1–16,243 main commentary; lines 16,261–17,205 are the Index of Sanskrit Words (reference glossary only, noted but not substantively read).** Read across multiple sessions spanning two conversations. Source file: `raw/Brahma Sutras (Vedanta Sutras) With Commen - George Thibaut.txt`.

**Key contributions — Rāmānuja's *Śrī-Bhāṣya***:

**Adhyāya I (Samanvaya)**:
- The *Mahāpūrvapakṣa*: Rāmānuja's seven-pronged attack on Advaita's coherence (*avidyā* cannot be defined, cannot locate Brahman as its locus, cannot have Brahman as its object; the self cannot be unknowing to itself; liberation as "knowledge" is circular).
- The formal definition of *śarīra* (body): "any substance a conscious being controls and supports for its own purposes, and which stands in entirely subordinate relation to that being." This is the hermeneutical foundation for reading identity-texts and difference-texts without Advaita's two truths.
- *Tat tvam asi* ("that thou art") = inseparable dependence as body to soul, not absolute identity.

**Adhyāya II (Avirodha)**:
- The *arambhaṇa* adhikaraṇa: world = Brahman's real *pariṇāma* (transformation), not *vivarta* (appearance). Decisive for *pariṇāmavāda* vs. *vivartavāda*.
- Soul's properties: knower (*jñātṛ*), agent (*kartṛ*), atomic (*aṇu*), part/mode (*aṃśa*) of Brahman. Plural, not one.
- Refutation of Sāṃkhya, Vaiśeṣika, Buddhism (vijñānavāda, momentariness), Jainism (*syādvāda*), and Pāśupata; defense of Pāñcarātra and the *vyūha* doctrine.
- Cosmological sequence: Brahman → *ākāśa* → *vāyu* → fire → water → earth.
- The *antaryāmin*-Brāhmaṇa (Bṛhadāraṇyaka III,7): Brahman dwells within every entity including individual souls, ruling from within while remaining "the Immortal" untouched by what it inhabits.

**Adhyāya III (Sādhana)**:
- Transmigration: souls in plants are "conjunctively present" (not embodied); only from the *yoni* does the soul obtain a new retributive body.
- Dreams = created by the Supreme Person (prophetic; retributive); deep sleep = the soul rests in Brahman + the *nāḍīs*; same person wakes (memory, karma, scripture, liberation-injunctions all require it).
- Brahman's twofold character: (1) freedom from all imperfections AND (2) all blessed qualities; connexion with bodies is *līlā* for Brahman.
- *Neti neti* = denies exhaustiveness of any finite predicate, not the truth of attributes themselves.
- Unity of vidyās across *śākhās*; Udgitha vidyās are separate (different subject-matters); essential attributes (bliss, knowledge, infinity, freedom from evil) must be included in all Brahman-meditations.
- *Upāsanā* = *dhyāna* = *vedana*: the roots are interchangeable; liberating knowledge is repeated sustained practice, not a one-time event; must continue until death.
- Three auxiliaries: *paṇḍitya* (full mastery), *bālya* (not manifesting pride), *mauna* (reiterated vivid representation = upāsanā itself).
- Knowledge has an independent fruit (against Jaimini); three āśramas all obligatory; lapsed Naishṭhikas have no expiation; for householders, obligatory works are prerequisites that please the Lord for knowledge.

**Adhyāya IV (Phala)**:
- Three karman categories: *anārabdha-kārya* (destroyed at rise of knowledge), *ārabdha-kārya* (exhausted by retributive experience), post-knowledge karma (does not cling).
- Good works at death received by friends; evil works by enemies; Agnihotra still obligatory for the knower.
- Departure sequence: speech → mind; mind → *prāṇa*; *prāṇa* + soul → elements. Knower exits through the *suṣumnā* (100th+1 artery); assisted by the Lord's grace. Subtle body persists on the journey.
- Path of the Gods (*devayāna*): light → day → bright fortnight → northern solstice months → Vāyu → sun → lightning → Varuṇa → Indra → Prajāpati → Brahman. Conducting deities lead. Symbol-meditators excluded (*tatkratuḥ*).
- Release = manifestation of eternally existing true nature (not creation). Soul is non-divided from Brahman as body from its soul.
- Three views on soul's nature: Jaimini (Brahman-character), Audulomi (pure intelligence), Bādarāyaṇa (both — lump-of-salt analogy). Creates by mere will; self-ruler; body at will.
- World-ruling power (*jagat-vaiṣamya*) is the exclusive attribute of the Supreme Person; the released soul does not acquire it.
- Non-return is absolute (Kh VIII,15; BG VIII,15–16; BG VII,17–19): Brahman will not eject the Devotee it loves.

**Pages created (4)**: scholarship/thibaut-vedanta-sutras (major expansion), concepts/sarira-sariri, concepts/antaryami, concepts/upasana, concepts/path-of-the-gods.

**Pages updated (4)**: commentators/ramanuja (comprehensive expansion — full doctrine, hermeneutics, controversies), traditions/hinduism/sects/vishishtadvaita-vedanta (comprehensive expansion — full doctrinal tables, karman categories, state of release), index.md (thibaut scholarship entry updated to note full ingest; 4 new Hindu concepts added).

**Coverage: Full** (entire main commentary SBE Vol. 48, Adhyāyas I–IV, lines 1–16,243; Index of Sanskrit Words noted and set aside).

## 2026-06-10 ingest | Kirkland, Russell. *Taoism: The Enduring Tradition*. Routledge, 2004.

Source type: scholarly survey / revisionist history of religion. **Coverage: scope = full main body (Chs. 1–5 + Conclusion), lines 193–1849 — read in full.** Lines 1850+ are endnotes only (citation references). Source: `raw/scholarship/Taoism the enduring Tradition - Kirkland, Russell;Giradot, Norman J.;.txt`.

**Central arguments**:
1. The tao-chia/tao-chiao ("philosophical Taoism / religious Taoism") dichotomy is a **modern scholarly fabrication** — *tao-chia* was always a Han bibliographic category, not a self-designating community.
2. The true foundational Taoist text is the **Nei-yeh** (*Kuan-tzu* ch. 49, c. 4th c. BCE), not the Daodejing; it focuses purely on biospiritual cultivation and predates the received Tao te ching.
3. The continuous practical core of Taoism across all periods is **biospiritual cultivation** (ch'i / ching / shen / hsin) — not "wu-wei philosophy."
4. "Taoism" as an organized social community only emerged when **Lu Hsiu-ching** organized the first Taoist canon (~471 CE).
5. The Daodejing has three strata (Guodian / Ma-wang-tui / received) — the Huan Yüan hypothesis places the full-text redaction at the Chi-hsia academy.
6. The received 33-chapter Zhuangzi was assembled by **Kuo Hsiang** from a 52-chapter original; Kuo's confessional preface (discovered in Kyoto) admits he edited toward "what is easy to understand."
7. Ko Hung (often cited as representative of Taoism) was NOT representative — his elevation by Creel/Welch was ideologically motivated.
8. Taoism was deeply embedded in imperial legitimation, NOT anti-political.
9. Women had formal sacerdotal roles in T'ien-shih; their diminution in late imperial period reflects social change, not Taoist ideology.

## 2026-06-10 ingest | Adler, *Reconstructing the Confucian Dao: Zhu Xi's Appropriation of Zhou Dunyi* (SUNY, 2014)

Source type: scholarship — monograph. **Coverage: read in full (complete scope) — Introduction, Chapters 1–4 (including annotated Tongshu translation, Zhu Xi's published commentary Parts I and II), two key postfaces (1169 and 1179), Bibliography, Index. Lines 1–6,339.** Source file: `raw/scholarship/Reconstructing the Confucian Dao - Adler, Joseph Alan.txt`.

Key contributions:
1. **Adler's central argument**: Zhou Dunyi's role as "founder" of the Cheng-Zhu school was entirely Zhu Xi's retrospective construction (~100 years after Zhou died, 1169–1196). The conventional reason: Zhou's *taiji* concept provided the cosmological link between *qi* and *li*. The deeper reason (Adler's contribution): Zhou's theory of the **interpenetration of activity and stillness** (Tongshu section 16) cosmologically verified Zhu Xi's 1169 ji-chou enlightenment.
2. **Translation**: *Taiji* = "Supreme Polarity" (NOT "Supreme Ultimate"). *Ji* = ridgepole = the highest point AND the pivot-junction that divides the roof into complementary halves — i.e., it is BOTH the pinnacle AND the principle of bipolarity. *Wuji er taiji* = "Nonpolar and yet Supreme Polarity!"
3. **Three stages of Zhu Xi's zhong/he problem**: (1) 1153–1163 (Li Tong): stillness emphasis / quiet-sitting; (2) 1167–1168 (Zhang Shi): activity emphasis; (3) 1169 ji-chou enlightenment: interpenetration — stillness within activity, activity within stillness — joined by *jing* (reverent composure).
4. **Tongshu section 16** as the pivot: "That which has no activity in activity, and no stillness in stillness, is spirit" — the cosmological verification of Zhu Xi's practical resolution.
5. **Daotong**: Zhu Xi wrote 22 pieces promoting Zhou Dunyi 1169–1196. He replaced the prior account (Cheng Hao independently revived the Way) with Zhou Dunyi as the common teacher of both Chengs. Zhu Xi's use of *zheng tong* (normally reserved for imperial dynastic succession) as a deliberate political challenge to imperial claims of moral authority.
6. **Daoist diagram origins**: Chen Tuan → Chong Fang → Mu Xiu → Zhou Dunyi's circles. Zhu Xi denied this. The popular yin-yang swirl first appears c. 1370 in Zhao Huiqian — NOT from Zhou Dunyi.
7. **Zhu Xi as Benedict of Nursia**: Adler's comparison — Zhu was primarily concerned with facilitating sustainable religious practice (sagehood-cultivation), not speculative philosophy.

## [2026-06-27] ingest | The Incoherence of the Philosophers (al-Ghazali, trans. Marmura)

**Scope:** the whole treatise — Author's Introductions + the twenty Discussions + the Book's
Conclusion (Marmura parallel English–Arabic edition, body lines ~1262–9913). **Read in full**
via five deployed Sonnet subagents partitioned by Discussion-range (Disc 1; Disc 2–10; Disc
11–16; Disc 17; Disc 18–20 + Conclusion), reconciled on the main thread. Translator's
introduction and endnotes/bibliography/index = reference apparatus, consulted as needed, not
ingested as running content. Scope complete.

**New pages:** text [[incoherence-of-the-philosophers]] (+ stub [[incoherence-of-the-incoherence]]);
commentators [[al-ghazali]], [[avicenna]], [[al-farabi]] (+ stub [[averroes]]); sects
[[asharism]], [[falsafa]]; concepts [[kalam]], [[takfir]], [[zahir-batin]], [[occasionalism]],
[[emanation]], [[eternity-of-the-world]], [[divine-attributes-in-islam]],
[[gods-knowledge-of-particulars]], [[prophetic-miracle]], [[bodily-resurrection]],
[[soul-in-islamic-philosophy]]; controversy [[eternity-of-the-world]].

**Extended:** [[islam]] (added asharism + falsafa sects; the Tahafut reason/revelation section);
[[creation-ex-nihilo]] (Islamic *huduth* vs. eternal world).

**Key takeaways:** al-Ghazali attacks [[falsafa|falsafa]] (Avicenna, al-Farabi) on its *own*
demonstrative (*burhan*) terms, claiming its metaphysics is undemonstrated. He pronounces the
philosophers infidels (*[[takfir]]*) on exactly three doctrines — the world's pre-eternity,
God's ignorance of temporal particulars, and the denial of bodily resurrection — treating all
other errors (incl. causal necessity) as *bid'a* shared with the [[mutazilism|Mu'tazila]], not
*kufr*. The Seventeenth Discussion's denial of necessary causation ("fire does not burn… God
creates the burning") is the classic statement of Ash'arite [[occasionalism]]. Hermeneutics: the
*[[zahir-batin|zahir/batin]]* axis is decisive — Ghazali permits allegory only where reason
proves the literal impossible, forbidding it for resurrection. Answered by [[averroes|Averroes]]
in the *[[incoherence-of-the-incoherence|Tahafut al-Tahafut]]* (source present, not yet ingested).

**Bookkeeping:** `index.md` (texts, commentators, concepts, sects, controversies); `overview.md`
(Islamic-philosophy note); source filed to `raw/texts/`. Lint: all ingest red links/orphans
resolved (created [[zahir-batin]] to clear the one red link).

Pages created: `scholarship/zachhuber-rise-of-christian-theology.md` (source summary + scope plan + ledger), `concepts/ousia-hypostasis-distinction.md` (the classical theory — abstract/concrete accounts), `commentators/basil-of-caesarea.md`, `commentators/gregory-of-nyssa.md`, `commentators/gregory-of-nazianzus.md`, `commentators/eunomius.md`, `commentators/apollinarius-of-laodicea.md`.

Index updated (new Greek-Fathers/Cappadocians commentator subsection; Patristic Philosophy scholarship entry; Patristic Metaphysics concept subsection). Resolves prior red links `basil-of-caesarea`, `gregory-of-nyssa`, `gregory-of-nazianzus` (referenced from `cappadocian-fathers`).

Key takeaways: (1) Zachhuber reads patristic doctrine *as philosophy*; (2) the Cappadocian "classical theory" has two versions (Basil abstract / Gregory of Nyssa concrete), fused in *Epistle 38*; (3) **contra Lossky/Zizioulas**, the Cappadocian Trinity is an ontology of being-as-one continuous with Hellenism — the real metaphysical "revolution" came later from Christology (conceptualizing the individual *as individual*). Recorded as a scholarly position (not adjudicated) where it contests the personalist reading.

**Multi-scope work in progress** — raw source NOT yet filed out of `raw/` root (filing happens after the declared whole-work scope is complete). Scopes 2–6 pending; next pass = Ch. 3 (lines 2815–4477).

## 2026-06-28 ingest | Johannes Zachhuber, *The Rise of Christian Theology* — Scope 2 (Ch. 3)

Source: `raw/The rise of Christian theology and the end of ancient metaphysics ... (Johannes Zachhuber) ...txt` (OUP, 2020). Scope 2 = **Chapter 3, "The Rise of the Classical Theory and the Challenge of Christology" (lines 2882–4424) — read in full** on the main thread (sequential). Covers §3.1 (the classical theory becomes a *koine* — Cyril of Alexandria and Theodoret of Cyrus; §3.1.2 the Cappadocians as symbols of Christian *paideia*) and §3.2 (Christology's philosophical challenges — the classical theory's lack of an account of individuation; the Apollinarian debate and Gregory of Nyssa's implicit "abstract reduction" of *physis*; the double homoousion in Apollinarius/Nestorius/Cyril/Theodoret; the *two natures → two hypostases* inference; §3.2.3 Chalcedon as a crisis of the classical theory).

Pages created: `commentators/cyril-of-alexandria.md`, `commentators/theodoret-of-cyrus.md`, `commentators/nestorius.md`, `concepts/double-homoousion.md`, `controversies/chalcedonian-definition.md`.

Pages extended: `concepts/hypostatic-union.md` (patristic-philosophy background — the individuation problem, double homoousion, Chalcedon crisis; sources_ingested 1→2), `concepts/ousia-hypostasis-distinction.md` (5th-c. koine spread via Cyril/Theodoret; Christological-crisis fill-in), `scholarship/zachhuber-rise-of-christian-theology.md` (scope plan + ledger + Scope 2 pages). Index updated (Cappadocians commentator subsection extended; double-homoousion concept; chalcedonian-definition controversy subsection).

Key takeaways: (1) the Cappadocian "classical theory" became a shared *koine* by the early 5th c. — Cyril (Alexandrian) and Theodoret (Antiochene) both presuppose it despite opposed Christologies; (2) the theory had **no account of individuation**, making it ill-suited to Christology; (3) the double homoousion + the shared *two-natures-imply-two-hypostases* inference left **Chalcedon's "two natures in one hypostasis" philosophically unsupported** — the crisis that drove the rest of the book. Contradiction Protocol observed on `controversies/chalcedonian-definition.md` (three Christologies recorded, not adjudicated).

Forward references to "Severus of Antioch" left as plain text (page created in Scope 3). **Multi-scope work in progress** — raw source NOT yet filed. Next pass = Ch. 4 (Severus/miaphysitism) + Ch. 5 (Philoponus), lines 4425–6536.

Scopes 3–6 = **Chapters 4–10 (lines 4478–12417) — read in full**, completing the six-scope plan; the **entire body (Chs. 1–10) is now read in full**, Bibliography excluded as apparatus. Deployed-subagent strategy: four background Sonnet subagents read disjoint line-ranges (Scope 3 = 4478–6536, Chs. 4–5; Scope 4 = 6537–8231, Chs. 6–7; Scope 5 = 8232–10526, Ch. 8; Scope 6 = 10527–12417, Chs. 9–10) to scratchpad notes; the **main thread owned all taxonomy, page creation, and cross-linking**.

Pages created (11 commentators): `severus-of-antioch`, `john-philoponus`, `john-the-grammarian-of-caesarea`, `damian-of-alexandria`, `peter-of-callinicus`, `leontius-of-byzantium`, `pamphilus-the-theologian`, `theodore-of-raithu`, `leontius-of-jerusalem`, `maximus-the-confessor`, `john-of-damascus`; (1 concept) `concepts/enhypostaton.md`; (1 controversy) `controversies/tritheist-controversy.md`.

Pages extended: `concepts/ousia-hypostasis-distinction.md` (the three post-Chalcedonian transformations — "the hidden revolution"); `scholarship/zachhuber-rise-of-christian-theology.md` (ledger Scopes 3–6, pages-generated, early-modern afterlife). Index updated (post-Chalcedonian transformers commentator subsection; enhypostaton concept; tritheist-controversy; Zachhuber scholarship line marked complete).

Key takeaways: (1) Christology forced two rival reconstructions of the classical theory on the Chalcedonian side — the **philosophy of the enhypostaton** (Leontius of Byzantium → Pamphilus → Theodore of Raïthu; substance/accident binary, hypostasis marginalized) and the **philosophy of the hypostasis** (John the Grammarian → Leontius of Jerusalem → John of Damascus; hypostasis as the foundational individual in which all else inheres); (2) the miaphysite **particular natures** (Severus → Philoponus) drove the **tritheist controversy**; (3) **Maximus** alone tried to return to the classical theory (whole/part ontology); (4) **John of Damascus** ("End and Beginning") synthesized the Chalcedonian lines into a near-explicit essence/existence duality "deeply alien to ancient metaphysics," transmitted West via Burgundio/Peter Lombard — Zachhuber's "end of ancient metaphysics." Contradiction Protocol observed on `controversies/tritheist-controversy.md` and within commentator pages (Krausmüller vs. Zachhuber on Severus/Pamphilus; Elert's Theodore-of-Pharan identification; scholarly dating disputes) — recorded, not adjudicated.

**Work complete for the full declared scope.** Raw source filed: `git mv` from `raw/` root into `raw/scholarship/`.
